. The completion of the maps yields the desired equivalence.

As a consequence, we get the following equivalences between the left hearts:

\Omega_{\mathscr{L}(X)}\widetilde{\otimes}_{A}-:LH\left(\operatorname{Mod}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}c_{K}}\left(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(U)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(X)\right)\right)\leftrightarrows\\
LH\left(\operatorname{Mod}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}c_{K}}\left(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(U)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(X)^{\operatorname{op}}\right)\right):\Omega_{\mathscr{L}(X)}^{-1}\widetilde{\otimes}_{A}-.

In virtue of
[
9
, Proposition 4.31]
, we have the following canonical identifications of abelian categories:

LH\left(\operatorname{Mod}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}c_{K}}\left(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(U)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(X)\right)\right)\\
\cong LH\left(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}\left(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(U)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(X)\right)\right),

LH\left(\operatorname{Mod}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}c_{K}}\left(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(U)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(X)^{\operatorname{op}}\right)\right)\\
\cong LH\left(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}\left(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(U)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(X)^{\operatorname{op}}\right)\right).

A fortiori, we obtain an equivalence of abelian categories:

\Omega_{\mathscr{L}(X)}\widetilde{\otimes}_{A}-:LH\left(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}\left(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(U)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(X)\right)\right)\rightarrow\\
LH\left(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}\left(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(U)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(X)^{\operatorname{op}}\right)\right),

with inverse given by the following functor:

\Omega_{\mathscr{L}(X)}^{-1}\widetilde{\otimes}_{A}-:LH\left(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}\left(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(U)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(X)^{\operatorname{op}}\right)\right)\rightarrow\\
LH\left(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}\left(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(U)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(X)\right)\right).

We need to show that this induces an equivalence of quasi-abelian categories between the associated categories of Ind-Banach modules.

However, as
\Omega_{\mathscr{L}(X)}
and
\Omega_{\mathscr{L}(X)}^{-1}
are finite projective
A
-modules, they are a direct summand in a finite-free
A
-module. Therefore, the functor
\Omega_{\mathscr{L}(X)}\widetilde{\otimes}_{A}-
maps Ind-Banach spaces to Ind-Banach spaces, and so maps the essential image of:

\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}\left(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(U)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(X)\right)

inside its left heart to
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}\left(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(U)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(X)^{\operatorname{op}}\right)
. As the analogous phenomena holds for
\Omega_{\mathscr{L}(X)}^{-1}\widetilde{\otimes}_{A}-
, the result holds.
∎

Notice that the procedure above is functorial. Recall the following notation:

Definition
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a morphism of rigid analytic spaces. Then we define the derived pullback along
f
to be the following functor:

\displaystyle f^{*}:\operatorname{D}(

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{Y})\rightarrow\operatorname{D}(\mathcal{O}_{X}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\mapsto\mathcal{O}_{X}\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{Y}}f^{-1}\mathcal{M}

We may use this to show the following proposition:

Proposition 6.1.6
.

Let
X
be a separated smooth rigid variety equipped with a Lie algebroid
\mathscr{L}
. There is a pair of mutually inverse equivalences of quasi-abelian categories:

p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}-:\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))\leftrightarrows\\
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})):p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}^{-1}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}-.

Proof.

First, notice that by definition
\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}
is a line bundle in
X
. In particular, both
p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}
and
p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}^{-1}
are concentrated in degree zero, so the above functors make sense. Next, we may assume that
X=\operatorname{Sp}(A)
is affinoid and
\mathscr{L}
is free. In this situation, both
\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}
and
\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}^{-1}
are globally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules. In particular, for every
\mathcal{M}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))
, and every affinoid subdomain
V\subset X^{2}
we have the following identity:

\left(p_{2}^{-1}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{M}\right)(V)=\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}(p_{2}(V))\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(p_{2}(V))}\mathcal{M}(V).

For simplicity, let
\tilde{M}=p_{2}^{-1}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{M}
. In order to define an action of
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
on
\tilde{M}
, it suffices to define it at the level of presheaves. In particular, for every affinoid subdomain
V\subset X^{2}
we need to define an action:

\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}(V)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(p_{1}(V))\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\mathcal{O}_{X}(p_{2}(V))}\left(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{1}(V))\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{2}(V))^{\operatorname{op}}\right)\times\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(V)\\
\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(V),

functorial in
\mathcal{M}(V)\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{1}(V))\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{2}(V)))
, and
V\subset X^{2}
. As we mentioned above,
p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}
as an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}
-module. This isomorphism induces an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}
-modules
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\cong\mathcal{M}
. Thus, we reduce the problem to obtaining a functorial action:

\left(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{1}(V))\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{2}(V))^{\operatorname{op}}\right)\times\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(V)\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(V),

such that the restriction to
\mathcal{O}_{X}(p_{1}(V))\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\mathcal{O}_{X}(p_{2}(V))
agrees with the restriction of the action of
\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}(V)
on
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(V)
.

As
V
is affinoid,
p_{1}(V)
and
p_{2}(V)
are quasi-compact open subspaces of
X
. Thus,
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{1}(V))
, and
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{2}(V))
are Fréchet algebras. As before, we may apply
[
9
, Proposition 4.31]
to conclude that
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{1}(V))\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{2}(V))
is the image under
\operatorname{diss}:\widehat{\mathcal{B}}c_{K}\rightarrow\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})
of the complete bornological
K
-algebra:

\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{1}(V))\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{2}(V)).

Thus, every object in
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{1}(V))\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{2}(V)))
is the cokernel of a strict morphism of complete bornological
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{1}(V))\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{2}(V))
-modules. In particular, there is a direct sum of Banach spaces
\bigoplus_{i\in I}W_{i}
such that there is a strict epimorphism:

\left(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{1}(V))\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{2}(V))\right)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\bigoplus_{i}W_{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(V).

Hence, we may replace
\mathcal{M}
with
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\bigoplus_{i}W_{i}
, and assume that
\mathcal{M}
is a sheaf of complete bornological spaces (
cf
. the proof of
[
9
, Theorem 6.11]
). Again, freeness of
\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}
implies that
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
is a sheaf of complete bornological spaces.

Thus, we have reduced the problem to defining a bounded action:

\left(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{1}(V))\otimes_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{2}(V))^{\operatorname{op}}\right)\times\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(V)\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(V).

Unlike for arbitrary Ind-Banach spaces, the fact that
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(V)
is a bornological space implies that it has an underlying
K
-vector space. Hence, we may define an action by using the formulas in equations
(\ref{equation side-changing})
and
(\ref{equation 2 side-changing})
. We just need to show that this action is bounded. By definition of the projective tensor product of bornological spaces, it suffices to show that the actions of
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{1}(V))
and
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{2}(V))^{\operatorname{op}}
on

\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(V)
are bounded.

The fact that the action of
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{1}(V))
is bounded follows as in Lemma
6.1.5
. For the action of
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{2}(V))^{\operatorname{op}}
, choose a finite cover
\left(V_{i}\right)_{i=1}^{n}
satisfying the conditions from Lemma
6.1.4
. By Theorem
5.3.1
, together with functoriality of equation
(\ref{equation side-changing})
, we have the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{1}(V))\times\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i=1}^{n}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{1}(V_{i}))\times\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(V_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(V_{i})}

where the vertical arrows are strict injections, and horizontal arrows are the action given by
(\ref{equation side-changing})
. By Lemma
6.1.5
the lower horizontal map is bounded. Hence, as the vertical arrows are strict injections, it follows that the upper horizontal map is also bounded. After sheafification and completion, it follows that
\tilde{M}
is a module in
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
. The construction of the other functor is completely analogous, and the fact that they are mutually inverse follows by Lemma
6.1.5
.
∎

Definition 6.1.7
.

We will use the following notation to denote the functors defined in the previous proposition:

\operatorname{S}:=p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}-:\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))\leftrightarrows\\
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})):p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}^{-1}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}-=:\operatorname{S}^{-1},

and we will call
\operatorname{S}
and
\operatorname{S}^{-1}
the side-switching operators for bimodules.

As the side-switching operators are equivalences of quasi-abelian categories, they are strongly exact. Hence, they induce an equivalence of triangulated categories:

\operatorname{S}:\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))\leftrightarrows\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})):\operatorname{S}^{-1}.

We can use this derived equivalence to extend the side switching operators to the respective left hearts. In particular, we define:

\operatorname{S}:LH(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})))\rightarrow LH(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))),

as the following composition:

LH(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})))\rightarrow\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))\xrightarrow{\operatorname{S}}\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))\\
\xrightarrow{\operatorname{H}^{0}}LH(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))),

and
\operatorname{S}^{-1}:LH(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})))\rightarrow LH(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})))
analogously.

Proposition 6.1.8
.

The functors:

\operatorname{S}:LH(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})))\leftrightarrows LH(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))):\operatorname{S}^{-1},

are mutually inverse equivalences of abelian categories.

Proof.

This is a consequence of
[
43
, Proposition 1.2.34]
.
∎

Notice that, by construction, we have a commutative diagram:

(36)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{S}:LH(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces LH(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))):\operatorname{S}^{-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{S}:\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})):\operatorname{S}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}

We will now construct an analogous side-switching operation for right
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
-modules. First, we notice that we have the following identities:

\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})^{\operatorname{op}}=\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{p_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}}\left(p_{1}^{-1}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}\right),\\
\quad\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}=\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{p_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}}\left(p_{1}^{-1}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})\right).

Where the Ind-Banach algebra structure on

\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})^{\operatorname{op}}
extends the ones on
\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}
and
p_{1}^{-1}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}
, and the same holds for
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}
. Thus, mimicking the arguments for left modules, we may define side-switching operations for right modules in the following manner:

\displaystyle\operatorname{S}_{r}:=p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}^{-1}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}-:\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})^{\operatorname{op}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{S}_{r}^{-1}:=p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}-:\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})^{\operatorname{op}}).

The fact that these functors are well-defined mutually inverse equivalences of quasi-abelian categories is analogous to Proposition
6.1.6
.

There is yet another pair of side switching operators which are relevant to our setting. Namely, by definition,

\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})
is the sheaf of complete enveloping algebras of the Lie algebroid
\mathscr{L}^{2}
on
X^{2}
. Thus, the appropriate version of
[
9
, Theorem 6.11]
, yields an equivalence of quasi-abelian categories:

\Omega_{\mathscr{L}^{2}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}-:\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))\leftrightarrows\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})^{\operatorname{op}}):\Omega^{-1}_{\mathscr{L}^{2}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}-.

This equivalence, together with the operations defined above, allows us to obtain a side-switching operation for Ind-Banach
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
-modules. Namely, we have the following equivalence of quasi-abelian categories:

\operatorname{S}_{r}\left(\Omega_{\mathscr{L}^{2}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}\operatorname{S}^{-1}(-)\right):\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))\rightarrow\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}).

We will now obtain an explicit description of this equivalence:

Lemma 6.1.9
.

There is an isomorphism of complete bornological
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})^{\operatorname{op}}
-modules:

\Omega_{\mathscr{L}^{2}}=\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}\overrightarrow{\boxtimes}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}=p_{1}^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}.

Proof.

We can assume that
X
is affine and
\mathscr{L}
is free, in which case the proof of
[
52
, Tag 0FMA]
carries over to this setting without major changes.
∎

Proposition 6.1.10
.

Consider the following functor:

\operatorname{T}:=\left(\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}\overrightarrow{\boxtimes}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}^{-1}\right)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}-:\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))\rightarrow\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}).

Then there is a canonical natural equivalence of functors:

\operatorname{T}\xrightarrow{\cong}\operatorname{S}_{r}\left(\Omega_{\mathscr{L}^{2}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}\operatorname{S}^{-1}(-)\right).

In particular,
\operatorname{T}
is an equivalence of quasi-abelian categories.

Proof.

This is a routine calculation.
∎

As before, the fact that
\operatorname{T}
is an equivalence of quasi abelian categories implies that it is strongly exact. Hence, it extends to the derived category. For simplicity, we will also denote this extension by
\operatorname{T}
. Recall from Lemma
5.5.2
that the complex

\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})
is canonically an object in
\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}})
. The explicit description of
\operatorname{T}
given above allows us to show the following corollary:

Corollary 6.1.11
.

There is an isomorphism in
\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}})
:

\operatorname{T}(\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}))=\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}).

Proof.

By construction of
\operatorname{T}
, we have the following identities:

\displaystyle\operatorname{T}(\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})):=

\displaystyle p_{1}^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}\left(\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})\right)\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p_{1}^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}\left(\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{p^{-1}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{X}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}p^{-1}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{X}}\Delta_{*}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})\right)\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{p^{-1}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{X}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}p^{-1}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{X}}\Delta_{*}\left(\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{p^{-1}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{X}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}p^{-1}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{X}}\Delta_{*}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}),

where the third identity can be seen by applying
\Delta^{-1}
to both sides, the third one is analogous to
[
22
, Lemma 1.2.7]
, and the last one follows from the fact that the right
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
-module structure on
\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})
stems from the left
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}
-module structure on
\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}
, and both sheaves agree as sheaves of Ind-Banach spaces.
∎

This result will be instrumental to our study of Hochschild homology in the latter sections of the paper.

We conclude this section by studying the behavior of
the different derived functors discussed in Chapter
3

with respect to the side-switching operations:

Lemma 6.1.12
.

Consider a sheaf of Ind-Banach spaces
\mathcal{V}\in\operatorname{Shv}(X,\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K}))
, together with modules
\mathcal{M}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))
,
\mathcal{N}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
. There are canonical isomorphisms:

\operatorname{S}\left(\mathcal{M}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\mathcal{V}\right)=\operatorname{S}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\mathcal{V},\quad\operatorname{S}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{N}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\mathcal{V}\right)=\operatorname{S}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{N}\right)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\mathcal{V},

in
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
, and
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))
respectively.

Proof.

By construction we have the following identity in of sheaves of Ind-Banach spaces:

\operatorname{S}\left(\mathcal{M}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\mathcal{V}\right)=p_{2}^{-1}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}}\left(\mathcal{M}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\mathcal{V}\right)\\
=\left(p_{2}^{-1}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{M}\right)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\mathcal{V}=\operatorname{S}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\mathcal{V}.

We just need to show that these identifications are
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
-linear, which can be done locally. Thus, we may assume that
\mathcal{M}(X)
and
\mathcal{V}(X)
are complete bornological spaces, and then apply
(\ref{equation side-changing})
.
∎

Lemma 6.1.13
.

Choose
\mathcal{V}^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}(\operatorname{Shv}(X,LH(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}c_{K})))
, along with
\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))
, and
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
. There are canonical quasi-isomorphisms:

\operatorname{S}\left(\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{K}\mathcal{V}^{\bullet}\right)=\operatorname{S}\left(\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}\right)\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{K}\mathcal{V}^{\bullet},\quad\operatorname{S}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{K}\mathcal{V}^{\bullet}\right)=\operatorname{S}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}\right)\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{K}\mathcal{V}^{\bullet},

in
\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
, and
\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))
respectively.

Proof.

We show the first isomorphism. By construction we have:

\displaystyle\operatorname{S}\left(\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{K}\mathcal{V}^{\bullet}\right)=

\displaystyle\operatorname{S}\left(\operatorname{Tot}_{\oplus}\left(I(\mathcal{M}^{\bullet})\widetilde{\otimes}^{\bullet,\bullet}_{K}I(\mathcal{V}^{\bullet})\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tot}_{\oplus}\left(\operatorname{S}\left(I(\mathcal{M}^{\bullet})\widetilde{\otimes}^{\bullet,\bullet}_{K}I(\mathcal{V}^{\bullet})\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tot}_{\oplus}\left(\operatorname{S}\left(I(\mathcal{M}^{\bullet})\right)\widetilde{\otimes}^{\bullet,\bullet}_{K}I(\mathcal{V}^{\bullet})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tot}_{\oplus}\left(I\left(\operatorname{S}(\mathcal{M}^{\bullet})\right)\widetilde{\otimes}^{\bullet,\bullet}_{K}I(\mathcal{V}^{\bullet})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{S}\left(\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}\right)\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{K}\mathcal{V}^{\bullet},

where the third identity follows by Lemma
6.1.12
, together with diagram
(\ref{equation extension of S to the left heart})
.
∎

Proposition 6.1.14
.

There are natural equivalences:

R\underline{\mathcal{H}om}_{\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})}(-,-)\xrightarrow{\cong}R\underline{\mathcal{H}om}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})}(\operatorname{S}(-),\operatorname{S}(-)),\\
R\underline{\mathcal{H}om}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})}(-,-)\xrightarrow{\cong}R\underline{\mathcal{H}om}_{\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})}(\operatorname{S}^{-1}(-),\operatorname{S}^{-1}(-)).

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{V}^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}(\operatorname{Shv}(X,LH(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}c_{K})))
,
\mathcal{M}^{\bullet},\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))
. Then we have:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{D}(\operatorname{Shv}(X,LH(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}c_{K})))}

\displaystyle(\mathcal{V}^{\bullet},R\underline{\mathcal{H}om}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})}(\operatorname{S}(\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}),\operatorname{S}(\mathcal{N}^{\bullet})))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))}(\operatorname{S}(\mathcal{M}^{\bullet})\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{V}^{\bullet},\operatorname{S}(\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))}(\operatorname{S}\left(\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{V}^{\bullet}\right),\operatorname{S}(\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))}(\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{V}^{\bullet},\mathcal{N}^{\bullet})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{D}(\operatorname{Shv}(X,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}c_{K}))}(\mathcal{V}^{\bullet},R\underline{\mathcal{H}om}_{\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})}(\mathcal{M}^{\bullet},\mathcal{N}^{\bullet})),

and the result follows by Yoneda. The second isomorphism is analogous.
∎

We will now show the analogous results for derived tensor products:

Lemma 6.1.15
.

Choose
\mathcal{V}^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}(\operatorname{Shv}(X,LH(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}c_{K})))
, and
\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})^{\operatorname{op}})
, and
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}})
. There are canonical quasi-isomorphisms:

R\underline{\mathcal{H}om}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\mathcal{M}^{\bullet},\mathcal{V}^{\bullet})=\operatorname{S}^{-1}R\underline{\mathcal{H}om}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\operatorname{S}_{r}(\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}),\mathcal{V}^{\bullet}),\\
R\underline{\mathcal{H}om}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\mathcal{N}^{\bullet},\mathcal{V}^{\bullet})=\operatorname{S}R\underline{\mathcal{H}om}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\operatorname{S}_{r}^{-1}(\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}),\mathcal{V}^{\bullet}),

in
\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))
and
\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
respectively.

Proof.

The proof is dual to the proofs of lemmas
6.1.12
, and
6.1.13
.
∎

Proposition 6.1.16
.

There are natural equivalences:

-\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})}-=\operatorname{S}_{r}(-)\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})}\operatorname{S}(-),\textnormal{ }-\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})}-=\operatorname{S}_{r}^{-1}(-)\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})}\operatorname{S}^{-1}(-).

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{V}^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}(\operatorname{Shv}(X,LH(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}c_{K})))
,
\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})^{\operatorname{op}})
, and
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))
. Then we have:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{D}(\operatorname{Shv}(X,LH(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}c_{K})))}(\operatorname{S}_{r}(\mathcal{M})

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})}\operatorname{S}(\mathcal{N}),\mathcal{V}^{\bullet})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))}(\operatorname{S}(\mathcal{N}),R\underline{\mathcal{H}om}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\operatorname{S}_{r}(\mathcal{M}),\mathcal{V}))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))}(\mathcal{N},R\underline{\mathcal{H}om}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{V}))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{D}(\operatorname{Shv}(X,LH(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}c_{K})))}(\mathcal{M}\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})}\mathcal{N},\mathcal{V}^{\bullet}),

where we are using Lemma
6.1.15
. Thus, the result holds by Yoneda.
∎

In order to keep the notation reasonable, we introduce the following definition:

Definition 6.1.17
.

We define the immersion functor as the following composition:

\Delta_{*}^{\operatorname{S}}:=\operatorname{S}^{-1}\circ\Delta^{E}_{*}:\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})^{e})\rightarrow\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})).

We also have a version for right modules:

\Delta_{*}^{\operatorname{S}_{r}}:=\operatorname{S}_{r}^{-1}\circ\Delta^{E}_{*}:\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})^{e,\operatorname{op}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})^{\operatorname{op}}).

Analogously, we can define versions for the left hearts, which we denote by
\Delta_{*}^{I(\operatorname{S})}
, and
\Delta_{*}^{I(\operatorname{S})_{r}}
, and we denote their left derived functors by
\mathbb{L}\Delta_{*}^{\operatorname{S}}
and
\mathbb{L}\Delta_{*}^{\operatorname{S}_{r}}
respectively.

Notice that as the side-switching functor
\operatorname{S}
is an equivalence of quasi-abelian categories, we get a factorization:

\mathbb{L}\Delta_{*}^{\operatorname{S}}=\operatorname{S}^{-1}\circ\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}:\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})^{e})\rightarrow\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})),

and similarly for
\mathbb{L}\Delta_{*}^{\operatorname{S}_{r}}
. As a consequence, we get the following corollary:

Corollary 6.1.18
.

Let
\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})^{e})
. The following identities hold:

(i)

\mathcal{HH}^{\bullet}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}),\mathcal{M}^{\bullet})=\Delta^{-1}R\underline{\mathcal{H}om}_{\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})}(\mathbb{L}\Delta_{*}^{\operatorname{S}}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}),\mathbb{L}\Delta_{*}^{\operatorname{S}}\mathcal{M}^{\bullet})
.

(ii)

\mathcal{HH}_{\bullet}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}),\mathcal{M}^{\bullet})=\Delta^{-1}\left(\mathbb{L}\Delta_{*}^{\operatorname{S}_{r}}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})}\mathbb{L}\Delta_{*}^{\operatorname{S}}\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}\right)
.

Proof.

Identity
(i)
follows by Proposition
6.1.14
,
(ii)
follows by Proposition
6.1.16
.
∎

6.2.
Co-admissible modules over the bi-enveloping algebra I: The local case

As we will see, the sheaf of Ind-Banach algebras
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
is locally Fréchet-Stein. Hence, it is a natural to wonder if there could be a well-behaved category of co-admissible
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
-modules. This section is devoted to defining such a category, and showing some of its most important features.

As discussed above, the category of Ind-Banach
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
-modules is equivalent to the category of Ind-Banach
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})
-modules. Hence, the idea is using this equivalence to derive the properties of co-admissible
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
-modules from those of co-admissible
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})
-modules. Let
X
be a smooth affinoid space with a Lie algebroid
\mathscr{L}
, and assume
\mathscr{L}
admits a basis of global sections over
\mathcal{O}_{X}
. As seen in Section
5.4
, such a choice induces an isomorphism of sheaves of Ind-Banach algebras:

\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}:\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})\rightarrow\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}).

As a first consequence of this, we can show the following corollary:

Corollary 6.2.1
.

For any affinoid subdomain
V\subset X^{2}
, the Ind-Banach algebra
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)
is a Fréchet-Stein algebra.

Proof.

As
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})
is the sheaf of Fréchet-stein enveloping algebras of the Lie algebroid
\mathscr{L}^{2}
, it follows that
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(V)
is a Fréchet-Stein algebra. Thus, the isomorphism
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(V)\rightarrow\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)
makes the latter a Fréchet-Stein algebra.
∎

Assume that
X=\operatorname{Sp}(A)
, and let
\mathfrak{X}=\operatorname{Spf}(\mathcal{A})
be an affine formal model of
X
, and
\mathcal{L}
be a free
\mathcal{A}
-Lie lattice of
L=\mathscr{L}(X)
. Choose a basis of
\mathcal{L}
as an
\mathcal{A}
-module
x_{1},\cdots,x_{d}
. For later use, it will be convenient to construct an explicit Fréchet-Stein presentation of the algebra
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(X)
:

Proposition 6.2.2
.

For
n,m\geq 0
there is an isomorphism of Banach
K
-algebras:

\widehat{U}(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L})_{K}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m}\mathcal{L})_{K}\rightarrow\widehat{U}(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L}\times\pi^{m}\mathcal{L})_{K}.

Thus, we have the following chain of isomorphisms of Ind-Banach
K
-algebras:

\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(X^{2})=\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(X)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(X)=\varprojlim_{s}\left(\widehat{U}(\pi^{n+s}\mathcal{L})_{K}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m+s}\mathcal{L})_{K}\right),

and the latter inverse limit is a Fréchet-Stein presentation of
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(X^{2})
.

Proof.

By construction, for each
n,m\geq 0
we have a map of filtered
\mathcal{R}
-algebras:

U(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}U(\pi^{m}\mathcal{L})\rightarrow U(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L}\times\pi^{m}\mathcal{L}).

Taking the
\pi
-adic completion of both sides, we arrive at a map of
\mathcal{R}
-algebras:

\varphi_{n,m}:\widehat{U}(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L})\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m}\mathcal{L})\rightarrow\widehat{U}(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L}\times\pi^{m}\mathcal{L}).

It is enough to show that this is an isomorphism of complete
\mathcal{R}
-algebras. By Corollary
5.2.8
, for any
r\geq 0
, we have the following identities:

\displaystyle\widehat{U}(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L}\times\pi^{m}\mathcal{L})/\pi^{r}=

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{s}\left(\widehat{F}_{s}\left(U(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}U(\pi^{m}\mathcal{L})\right)/\pi^{r}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{s}\left(F_{s}\left(U(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}U(\pi^{m}\mathcal{L})\right)/\pi^{r}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{s}\left(F_{s}\left(U(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}U(\pi^{m}\mathcal{L})\right)\right)/\pi^{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle U(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}U(\pi^{m}\mathcal{L})/\pi^{r}.

Therefore,
\varphi_{n,m}
is an isomorphism of complete
\mathcal{R}
-algebras. Additionally, there is a canonical isomorphism of Banach
K
-algebras:

\left(\widehat{U}(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L})\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m}\mathcal{L})\right)\otimes_{\mathcal{R}}K=\widehat{U}(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L})_{K}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m}\mathcal{L})_{K}.

Combining these two facts, we get our desired isomorphism. The second part of the proposition follows by
5.3.7
, together with the fact that:

\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(X)=\varprojlim_{s}\widehat{U}(\pi^{n+s}\mathcal{L}\times\pi^{m+s}\mathcal{L})_{K},

is a Fréchet-Stein presentation.
∎

Corollary 6.2.3
.

There is a Fréchet-Stein presentation:

\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(X^{2})=\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(X)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(X)^{\operatorname{op}}=\varprojlim_{s}\left(\widehat{U}(\pi^{n+s}\mathcal{L})_{K}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m+s}\mathcal{L})_{K}^{\operatorname{op}}\right).

Proof.

In virtue of Proposition
5.4.3
, for each
n,m\geq 0
we have the following commutative diagram of morphisms of Banach
K
-algebras:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{U}(\pi^{n+1}\mathcal{L})_{K}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m+1}\mathcal{L})_{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{U}(\pi^{n+1}\mathcal{L})_{K}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m+1}\mathcal{L})_{K}^{\operatorname{op}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{U}(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L})_{K}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m}\mathcal{L})_{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{U}(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L})_{K}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m}\mathcal{L})_{K}^{\operatorname{op}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Id}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{t}_{m+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Id}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{t}_{m}}

By proposition
5.4.3
, the horizontal maps are isomorphisms of Banach
K
-algebras, and the leftmost vertical map is flat and has dense image by Proposition
6.2.2
. Thus, the rightmost vertical map is also flat and has dense image. In particular, it follows that we have a Fréchet-Stein presentation:

\varprojlim_{s}\left(\widehat{U}(\pi^{n+s}\mathcal{L})_{K}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m+s}\mathcal{L})_{K}^{\operatorname{op}}\right).

Furthermore, we have the following chain of isomorphisms of Ind-Banach algebras:

\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(X^{2})\xrightarrow{\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(X^{2})=\varprojlim_{s}\left(\widehat{U}(\pi^{n+s}\mathcal{L})_{K}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m+s}\mathcal{L})_{K}\right)\\
\xrightarrow{}\varprojlim_{s}\left(\widehat{U}(\pi^{n+s}\mathcal{L})_{K}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m+s}\mathcal{L})_{K}^{\operatorname{op}}\right),

so the proposition follows.
∎

As a consequence of the previous discussion, it follows that there is a well defined category of co-admissible bimodules over
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(X^{2})
, and it makes sense to wonder if there could be a category of sheaves of co-admissible modules over
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
. In order to define and study such a category, we will analyze the behavior of the extension of scalars along the map
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}:\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})\rightarrow\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
.

Remark 6.2.4
.

We remark that all the following results have analogs for right modules, with the same proofs. Hence, to avoid repetition, we will only state and prove the results for left modules.

Definition 6.2.5
.

Let
f:\mathscr{A}\rightarrow\mathscr{B}
be a morphisms of sheaves of Ind-Banach algebras on a
G
-topological space
X
. We define the extension of scalars along
f
as the following functor:

\displaystyle f^{*}:\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\mathscr{A})\rightarrow\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\mathscr{B}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\mapsto f^{*}(\mathcal{M})=\mathscr{B}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathscr{A}}\mathcal{M},

and the restriction of scalars along
f
as the functor:

f_{*}:\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\mathscr{B})\rightarrow\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\mathscr{A}),

defined by regarding
\mathcal{M}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\mathscr{B})
as an
\mathscr{A}
-module via the canonical map:

\mathscr{A}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\mathcal{M}\xrightarrow{f\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\operatorname{Id}}\mathscr{B}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}.

As the restriction functor
f_{*}
does not change the underlying sheaf of Ind-Banach spaces, we will just write
\mathcal{N}
instead of
f_{*}\mathcal{N}
when no confusion is possible.

Notice that both the extension and restriction of scalars are functorial, in the sense that for a pair of maps of sheaves of Ind-Banach algebras
f:\mathscr{A}\rightarrow\mathscr{B}
, and
g:\mathscr{B}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
we have:

\left(gf\right)^{*}=g^{*}f^{*},\quad\left(gf\right)_{*}=f_{*}g_{*}.

It is also clear that
\operatorname{Id}^{*}=\operatorname{Id}_{*}=\operatorname{Id}
. Hence, if
f:\mathscr{A}\rightarrow\mathscr{B}
is an isomorphism of sheaves of bornological algebras, it follows that
f^{*}
is an equivalence of quasi-abelian categories. Furthermore, we have:

Proposition 6.2.6
.

Extension and restriction of scalars are adjoint. In particular, it
f:\mathscr{A}\rightarrow\mathscr{B}
is a morphism of sheaves of Ind-Banach
K
-algebras, then:

f^{*}:\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\mathscr{A})\leftrightarrows\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\mathscr{B}):f_{*},

is a pair of adjoint functors.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{M}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\mathscr{A})
and
\mathcal{N}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\mathscr{B})
. We define a map:

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathscr{B}}(f^{*}\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathscr{A}}(\mathcal{M},f_{*}\mathcal{N}),

by sending a map
\varphi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathscr{B}}(f^{*}\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N})
to the composition:

\mathcal{M}\cong\mathscr{A}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathscr{A}}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathscr{B}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathscr{A}}\mathcal{M}\xrightarrow{\varphi}\mathcal{N}.

For the converse, we define a map
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathscr{A}}(\mathcal{M},f_{*}\mathcal{N})\rightarrow Hom_{\mathscr{B}}(f^{*}\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N})
, by sending
\psi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathscr{A}}(\mathcal{M},f_{*}\mathcal{N})
to the map:

f^{*}\mathcal{M}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Id}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathscr{A}}\psi}\mathscr{B}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathscr{A}}\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\mathcal{N},

where the last map is the action of
\mathscr{B}
on
\mathcal{N}
. A standard calculation shows these maps are mutually inverse.
∎

We will now study the image of the category of co-admissible modules
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))
under the extension of scalars
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}
. As above, there are Fréchet-Stein presentations:

\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(X^{2})=\varprojlim_{s}\left(\widehat{U}(\pi^{n+s}\mathcal{L})_{K}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m+s}\mathcal{L})_{K}^{\operatorname{op}}\right),\\
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(X^{2})=\varprojlim_{s}\left(\widehat{U}(\pi^{n+s}\mathcal{L})_{K}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m+s}\mathcal{L})_{K}\right).

To keep the notation simple, for each
n,m\geq 0
we will define the following algebras:

\widehat{E}_{n,m}:=\widehat{U}(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L})_{K}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m}\mathcal{L})_{K}^{\operatorname{op}},\quad\widehat{U}^{2}_{n,m}:=\widehat{U}(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L})_{K}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m}\mathcal{L})_{K}.

Similarly, we write
\wideparen{E}=\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(X^{2})
, and
\wideparen{U}^{2}=\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(X^{2})
. For each co-admissible
\wideparen{U}^{2}
-module
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{U})
, we set
\mathcal{M}_{n,m}:=\widehat{U}^{2}_{n,m}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}^{2}}\mathcal{M}
. In particular, we have:

\mathcal{M}=\varprojlim_{n,m}\mathcal{M}_{n,m}.

Let us start with the following lemma:

Lemma 6.2.7
.

For
n,m\geq 0
, there is an isomorphism of Banach
\wideparen{E}
-modules:

\widehat{E}_{n,m}=\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}\widehat{U}_{n,m}.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.4.3
, we have a commutative diagram of Fréchet
K
-algebras:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wideparen{U}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wideparen{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{U}^{2}_{n,m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{E}_{n,m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{n,m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{n,m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Id}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{T}_{n}}

By the properties of the functor
-\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{U}}-
, we obtain an
\wideparen{E}
-linear morphism of Ind-Banach spaces:

\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}\widehat{U}_{n,m}:=\wideparen{E}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{U}}\widehat{U}^{2}_{n,m}\rightarrow\widehat{E}_{n,m}.

We need to show it is an isomorphism. It is enough to show that the sequence:

\wideparen{E}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}_{n,m}^{2}\rightarrow\wideparen{E}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}_{n,m}^{2}\rightarrow\widehat{E}_{n,m}\rightarrow 0,

is strictly right exact. Thus, it suffices to show that
\widehat{E}_{n,m}
satisfies the universal property of the cokernel of the map:

\wideparen{E}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}_{n,m}^{2}\rightarrow\wideparen{E}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}_{n,m}^{2}.

However, this follows by the fact that the upper and lower horizontal maps in the commutative diagram above are isomorphisms.
∎

Proposition 6.2.8
.

For any affinoid admissible open
V\subset X^{2}
, there is a pair of mutually inverse equivalences of abelian categories:

\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}:\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(V))\leftrightarrows\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)):\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}.

Proof.

We will show this equivalence for
V=X^{2}
, the general case is analogous. As
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}
is an isomorphism, we have mutually inverse equivalences:

\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}:\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}^{2})\leftrightarrows\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}):\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}.

Thus, it suffices to show that
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}
and
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}
send co-admissible modules to co-admissible modules. Let then
M\in\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{U}^{2})
be a co-admissible module. For each
n,m\geq 0
, define the following Banach
\widehat{E}_{n,m}
-module:

\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}M_{n,m}:=\widehat{E}_{n,m}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{E}}\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}M.

We have the following chain of identities:

\displaystyle\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}M_{n,m}=\widehat{E}_{n,m}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{E}}\wideparen{E}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{U}^{2}}M=

\displaystyle\widehat{E}_{n,m}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{U}^{2}}M

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{E}_{n,m}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\widehat{U}^{2}_{n,m}}\left(\widehat{U}^{2}_{n,m}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{U}^{2}}M\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{E}_{n,m}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\widehat{U}^{2}_{n,m}}M_{n,m},

where the last identity follows by
[
9
, Corollary 5.40]
. As
M
is a co-admissible
\wideparen{U}^{2}
-module, each of the
M_{n,m}
is a finite module over
\wideparen{U}^{2}_{n,m}
. Hence,
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}M_{n,m}
is a finite module over
\widehat{E}_{n,m}
.
Furthermore, we have:

\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}M=\wideparen{E}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{U}^{2}}(\varprojlim M_{n,m})=\varprojlim\wideparen{E}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{U}^{2}}M_{n,m}=\varprojlim\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}M_{n,m},

where the second identity follows by the fact that
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}
is an equivalence, so it commutes with inverse limits, and the last one
follows by the isomorphism:

\widehat{E}_{n,m}=\wideparen{E}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{U}^{2}}\widehat{U}^{2}_{n,m}.

The corresponding fact for
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}
is analogous.
∎

Let
W\subset V\subset X^{2}
be affinoid subdomains. We have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}}

By
[
2
, Theorem 4.2.8]
, the leftmost vertical map is
c
-flat. Furthermore, there are Fréchet-Stein presentations:

\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(V))=\varprojlim_{n}A_{n},\textnormal{ }\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(W))=\varprojlim_{n}B_{n},

satisfying that for each
n\geq 0
the restriction map
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(V))\rightarrow\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(W))
induces a map
A_{n}\rightarrow B_{n}
which is two-sided flat. As the horizontal maps are isomorphisms of Fréchet-Stein algebras, it follows that
the restriction map
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V))\rightarrow\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W))
is
c
-flat as well.

Let
(W_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
be an affinoid cover of
V
. As
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}:\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})\rightarrow\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
is an isomorphism, and
V
is affinoid,
[
3
, Theorem 8.2]
shows that the augmented Čech complex:

(37)

0\rightarrow\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)\rightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{n}\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W_{i})\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(\cap_{i=1}^{n}W_{i})\rightarrow 0,

is a strict and exact complex. Let
M\in\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V))
be a co-admissible module and consider the following chain complex:

(38)

0\rightarrow M\rightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{n}\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W_{i})\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)}M\rightarrow\\
\cdots\rightarrow\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(\cap_{i=1}^{n}W_{i})\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)}M\rightarrow 0.

As
(\ref{equation augmented cech complex of bi-enveloping algebra})
is a bounded complex with
c
-flat transition maps,
[
9
, Proposition 5.33]
shows that
(\ref{equation augmented cech complex of co-admissible module over bi-enveloping algebra})
is strictly exact. Furthermore, for
1\leq i_{1},\cdots,i_{j}\leq n
, the Fréchet module
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(\cap_{k=1}^{j}W_{i_{k}})\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)}M
is a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(\cap_{k=1}^{j}W_{i_{k}})
-module.

As
V
is an affinoid space, it is separated. Hence, in order to define a sheaf on the admissible
G
-topology, it is enough to define it on the site of affinoid subspaces with finite covers. Thus, we obtain a functor:

\operatorname{Loc}:\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V))\rightarrow\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V}),

defined by assigning to each co-admissible module
M\in\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V))
, and each affinoid subdomain
W\subset V
, the co-admissible
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W)
-module:

\operatorname{Loc}(M)(W):=\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)}M.

We will refer to this functor as the localization functor.

Proposition 6.2.9
.

The following hold:

(i)

\operatorname{Loc}(-)
is a exact and fully faithful.

(ii)

If
W\subset V
is affinoid, then
\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M})(W)
is a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W)
-module.

(iii)

For each
M\in\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V))
, we have
\operatorname{H}^{j}(V,\operatorname{Loc}(M))=0
for all
j\geq 1
.

(iv)

For each
\mathcal{N}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V})
we have an isomorphism:

\operatorname{Hom}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V}}(\operatorname{Loc}(M),\mathcal{N})=\operatorname{Hom}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)}(M,\mathcal{N}(V)).

Proof.

Consider a short exact sequence of co-admissible
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)
-modules:

0\rightarrow M_{1}\rightarrow M_{2}\rightarrow M_{3}\rightarrow 0.

As this is a short exact sequence of Fréchet spaces, it is strict by the open mapping theorem. Let
W\subset V
be an affinoid subdomain. As shown above, the restriction map
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)\rightarrow\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W)
is
c
-flat. Thus, by
[
9
, Proposition 5.33]
, the sequence:

0\rightarrow\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)}M_{1}\rightarrow\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)}M_{2}\\
\rightarrow\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)}M_{3}\rightarrow 0,

is a strict exact sequence. Thus, it follows that:

0\rightarrow\operatorname{Loc}(M_{1})\rightarrow\operatorname{Loc}(M_{2})\rightarrow\operatorname{Loc}(M_{3})\rightarrow 0,

is a strict exact sequence in
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V})
. In order to show that
\operatorname{Loc}(-)
is fully faithful, it suffices to show
(iv)
. Let
f:\operatorname{Loc}(M)\rightarrow\mathcal{N}
be a morphism of
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
-modules. For any affinoid subdomain
W\subset V
there is a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}(W)}

Notice that by Proposition
6.2.6
, we have:

\operatorname{Hom}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)}(M,\mathcal{N}(W))=\operatorname{Hom}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W)}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)}M,\mathcal{N}(W)).

Thus,
f:\operatorname{Loc}(M)\rightarrow\mathcal{N}
is completely determined by the morphism
M\rightarrow\mathcal{N}(V)
induced on global sections. Conversely, any map
M\rightarrow\mathcal{N}(V)
induces a morphism
M\rightarrow\mathcal{N}(W)
by composing with the restriction maps. Again by extension of scalars, we get commutative diagrams as above, and these determine a unique morphism of sheaves
\operatorname{Loc}(M)\rightarrow\mathcal{N}
. Statement
(ii)
is
[
9
, Proposition 5.33]
, and
(iii)
follows by the fact that the higher Čech cohomology groups of
\operatorname{Loc}(M)
vanish for all finite affinoid covers, as was shown above.
∎

Definition 6.2.10
.

Let
V\subset X^{2}
be an admissible affinoid open. We define the category of co-admissible
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V}
-modules,
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V})
, as the essential image of the localization functor:

\operatorname{Loc}:\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V))\rightarrow\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V}).

We will now compare the co-admissible modules over
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V}
and
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})_{|V}
:

Lemma 6.2.11
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})_{|V})
be a sheaf of co-admissible modules. For any pair of affinoid subdomains
W\subset V\subset X^{2}
we have:

\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}\mathcal{M}(W)=\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)}\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}\mathcal{M}(V).

Proof.

By construction, we have:

\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}\mathcal{M}(W)=\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(W)}\mathcal{M}(W).

As
\mathcal{M}
is co-admissible, and
W
is affinoid, we have the following identity:

\mathcal{M}(W)=\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(W)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(V)}\mathcal{M}(V).

Combining these two identities, we get:

\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}\mathcal{M}(W)=\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(V)}\mathcal{M}(V)=\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(W)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)}\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}\mathcal{M}(V),

and we get our desired isomorphism.
∎

Proposition 6.2.12
.

For each admissible affinoid open
V\subset X^{2}
, we have mutually inverse equivalences of abelian categories:

\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}:\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})_{|V})\leftrightarrows\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V}):\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}.

Proof.

As
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}
and
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}
are mutually inverse equivalences of quasi-abelian categories between

\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})_{|V})
and
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V})
, it suffices to show that the essential image under
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}
of
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})_{|V})
is
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V})
. By Proposition
6.2.8
and Lemma
6.2.11
, we have a commutative diagram of functors:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(V))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(V))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})_{|V})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Loc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Loc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}}

the upper horizontal functor is an equivalence by Proposition
6.2.8
, and the vertical maps are equivalences by
[
3
, Theorem 8.4]
, and Proposition
6.2.9
. Hence, the lower horizontal map is also an equivalence.
∎

Moreover, we get a version of Kiehl’s Theorem for co-admissible
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
-modules:

Corollary 6.2.13
.

Let
V\subset X^{2}
be an admissible affinoid open and choose a module
\mathcal{M}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V})
. The following are equivalent:

(i)

\mathcal{M}
is a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}_{|V})
-module.

(ii)

There is an admissible affinoid cover
(U_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
of
V
such that:
\mathcal{M}_{|U_{i}}
is a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|U_{i}}
-module for each
1\leq i\leq n
.

(iii)

For any admissible affinoid cover
(U_{i})_{i\in I}
of
V
,
\mathcal{M}_{|U_{i}}
is a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|U_{i}}
-module for each
i\in I
.

Proof.

In virtue of Proposition
6.2.12
, it suffices to show the corresponding statement for co-admissible modules over
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})_{|V}
. This follows by Kiehl’s Theorem for co-admissible modules over the Fréchet-Stein enveloping algebra of a Lie algebroid shown in
[
3
, Proposition 8.4]
.
∎

Now that we have a well-defined theory of co-admissible
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
-modules, we may adapt the theory of
\mathcal{C}
-complexes from
[
9
]
to this setting. We remark that, even if the results from
[
9
, Section 6]

are only stated for
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}
-modules, the results also hold for co-admissible modules over any Lie algebroid.

Definition 6.2.14
.

Let
V\subset X^{2}
be an admissible affinoid open, and choose a Fréchet-Stein presentation
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)=\varprojlim_{n}A_{n}
. A complex
C^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V})
is called a
\mathcal{C}
-complex if it satisfies the following properties:

(i)

\operatorname{H}^{i}(C^{\bullet})\in\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V})
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(ii)

For any
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have:

A_{n}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)}\left(\operatorname{H}^{i}(C^{\bullet})(V)\right)=0,

for all but finitely many
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

We let
\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V})
be the full subcategory of
\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V})
given by all
\mathcal{C}
-complexes. Given a closed embedding
Y\rightarrow V
, we let
\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}^{Y}}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V})
be the full subcategory of
\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V})
given by the
\mathcal{C}
-complexes with cohomology supported on
Y
.

Remark 6.2.15
.

This definition does not depend on the presentation:

\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)=\varprojlim_{n}A_{n}.

Indeed, let
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)=\varprojlim_{m}B_{m}
be another Fréchet-Stein presentation. For any
m\in\mathbb{Z}
there is some
n\in\mathbb{Z}
such that the projection
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)\rightarrow B_{m}
factors as:

\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)\rightarrow A_{n}\rightarrow B_{m}.

Hence, the fact that condition
(ii)
in Definition
6.2.14
holds for a Fréchet-Stein presentation implies that it holds for every Fréchet-Stein presentation.

We can relate this notion to the category of
\mathcal{C}
-complexes over
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})
via the following proposition:

Proposition 6.2.16
.

For each admissible affinoid open
V\subset X^{2}
, we have mutually inverse equivalences of categories:

\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}:\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})_{|V})\leftrightarrows\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V}):\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}.

Proof.

We show the case
V=X^{2}
, the general case is analogous. Let
C^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
. As
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}
is strongly exact, we have
\operatorname{H}^{i}(\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}C^{\bullet})=\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}\operatorname{H}^{i}(C^{\bullet})
, which is a co-admissible
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})
-module for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
by
6.2.12
. As in the proof of Proposition
6.2.8
, for each
n,m\in\mathbb{Z}
we have:

\widehat{U}_{n,m}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{U}}\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}\operatorname{H}^{i}(C^{\bullet})(X^{2})=\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}\left(\widehat{E}_{n,m}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{E}}\operatorname{H}^{i}(C^{\bullet})(X^{2})\right).

As
C^{\bullet}
is a
\mathcal{C}
-complex, the right hand side is zero for all but finitely many
i\in\mathbb{Z}
. Thus,
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}C^{\bullet}
is a
\mathcal{C}
-complex of
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})
-modules, as wanted. The fact that
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}
sends
\mathcal{C}
-complexes to
\mathcal{C}
-complexes is analogous.
∎

Corollary 6.2.17
.

The following hold:

(i)

\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V})
is a triangulated subcategory of
\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V})
.

(ii)

Let
Y\rightarrow V
be a closed immersion. Then the equivalence of Proposition
6.2.16
restricts to an equivalence of triangulated categories:

\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}:\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}^{Y}}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})_{|V})\leftrightarrows\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}^{Y}}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V}):\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}.

Proof.

Statement
(i)
follows by the corresponding fact for
\mathcal{C}
-complexes over
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})
, which is shown in
[
9
, Proposition 6.4]
. Statement
(ii)
holds because
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}
and
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}
do not change the supports.
∎

6.3.
Co-admissible modules over the bi-enveloping algebra II: Globalization

In order to generalize the definition of co-admissible
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
-modules to the case where
X
is not necessarily affinoid and
\mathscr{L}
is not free, we will need to relate the functors
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}
and
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}
to the globally defined side-switching operations defined in Section
6.1
. In particular, keeping the assumptions of the previous section, we have the following proposition:

Proposition 6.3.1
.

There are canonical natural equivalences of functors:

\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}\simeq\operatorname{S}:=p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}-,\quad\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}\simeq\operatorname{S}^{-1}:=p_{2}^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{L}}^{-1}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}-.

Proof.

Let
V\subset X^{2}
be an affinoid subdomain, and
M\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(V))
. We need to obtain a canonical identification:

\Omega_{\mathscr{L}(p_{2}(V))}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(p_{2}(V))}M\rightarrow\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(V)}M,

which is functorial in
M
and
V
. As in the proof of Proposition
6.1.6
, we can freely assume that
M
is a complete bornological space. Consider the following bornological algebras:

\mathscr{A}(V)=\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{1}(V))\otimes_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{2}(V)),\\
\mathscr{B}(V)=\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})(p_{1}(V))\otimes_{K}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}(p_{2}(V)).

By Proposition
5.3.7
and definition
5.4.1
, we have the following identities of Ind-Banach algebras:

\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(V)=\operatorname{diss}(\widehat{\mathscr{A}}\,\,),\quad\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})(V)=\operatorname{diss}(\widehat{\mathscr{B}}\,).

As
M
is bornological, it follows by
[
9
, Proposition 4.3]
that we may regard
M
as a complete bornological
\widehat{\mathscr{A}}
-module. A fortiori, this restricts to a structure as an
\mathscr{A}
-module. In particular,
M
has an underlying
K
-vector space. Hence, we can use (a slight modification of) the discussion above Proposition
6.1.3
to show that there is a canonical identification of bornological
\mathscr{B}
-modules:

\mathscr{B}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}\mathcal{M}(V)=\Omega_{\mathscr{L}(p_{2}(V))}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(p_{2}(V))}\mathcal{M}(V).

Applying completion and the dissection functor, we obtain the identification we wanted. The fact that the isomorphism in Proposition
6.1.3
is functorial, together with functoriality of completion and dissection shows that this process is functorial in both
V\subset X^{2}
, and
M\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})(V))
, as we wanted to show.
∎

We will now extend the definition of co-admissible
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
-modules to the global case. For the rest of this section, we let
X
be a smooth and separated rigid space equipped with a Lie algebroid
\mathscr{L}
.

Let
(U_{i})_{i\in I}
be an admissible affinoid cover of
X
such that
\mathscr{L}_{|U_{i}}
is free for each
i\in I
, and let
U_{i}=\operatorname{Sp}(A_{i})
. In this situation, the family
\mathfrak{U}=(U_{i}\times U_{j})_{i,j\in I}
is an admissible affinoid cover of
X^{2}
. For each
i,j\in I
, the disjoint union
V_{ij}=U_{i}\cup U_{j}
is an affinoid space, and
\mathscr{L}
induces a unique Lie algebroid on
V_{ij}
. Namely, we define
\mathscr{L}_{V_{ij}}
as the unique Lie algebroid on
V_{ij}
induced by the Lie algebroids
\mathscr{L}_{|U_{i}}
and
\mathscr{L}_{|U_{j}}
on
U_{i}
, and
U_{j}
respectively. Notice that, in this situation,
V_{ij}
satisfies the conditions at the beginning of Section
6.2
. Hence, we have a well defined category sheaves of co-admissible modules over
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}_{V_{ij}})
. Furthermore, we have an open immersion:

U_{i}\times U_{j}\subset V_{ij}\times V_{ij}=V_{ij}^{2}.

Thus, we have a well-defined category
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}_{V_{ij}})_{|U_{i}\times U_{j}})
, as defined on
6.2.10
.

Lemma 6.3.2
.

There is an identification of sheaves of Ind-Banach algebras:

\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|U_{i}\times U_{j}}=\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}_{V_{ij}})_{|U_{i}\times U_{j}}.

Proof.

By construction, we have:

\displaystyle\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|U_{i}\times U_{j}}=

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{p_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}}\left(p_{1}^{-1}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}\right)\right)_{|U_{i}\times U_{j}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}\times U_{j}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{p_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{U_{2}}}\left(p_{1}^{-1}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}_{|U_{i}})\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}_{|U_{j}})^{\operatorname{op}}\right).

Similarly, we also have:

\displaystyle\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}_{V_{ij}})_{|U_{i}\times U_{j}}=

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{O}_{V_{ij}^{2}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{p_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{V_{ij}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{V_{ij}}}p_{1}^{-1}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}_{V_{ij}})\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}_{V_{ij}})^{\operatorname{op}}\right)_{|U_{i}\times U_{j}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}\times U_{j}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{p_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{U_{2}}}\left(p_{1}^{-1}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}_{{}_{V_{ij}}|U_{i}})\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}_{{}_{V_{ij}}|U_{j}})^{\operatorname{op}}\right).

Moreover, we have
\mathscr{L}_{{}_{V_{ij}}|U_{i}}=\mathscr{L}_{|U_{i}}
and
\mathscr{L}_{{}_{V_{ij}}|U_{j}}=\mathscr{L}_{|U_{j}}
, so the statement holds.
∎

Hence, for each pair of affinoid subdomains
U,V\subset X
such that the restrictions of
\mathscr{L}
to
U
and
V
are free, we have a well-defined category
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|U\times V})
. We may use this to show the following:

Lemma 6.3.3
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
. The following are equivalent:

(i)

There is an admissible affinoid cover
(U_{i})_{i\in I}
of
X
such that
\mathscr{L}_{|U_{i}}
is free for each
i\in I
and
\mathcal{M}_{|U_{i}\times U_{j}}
is a co-admissible
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|U_{i}\times U_{j}})
-module for each
i,j\in I
.

(ii)

For every admissible affinoid cover
(U_{i})_{i\in I}
of
X
such that
\mathscr{L}_{|U_{i}}
is free for each
i\in I
, we have that
\mathcal{M}_{|U_{i}\times U_{j}}
is a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|U_{i}\times U_{j}}
-module for each
i,j\in I
.

Proof.

Assume there is an admissible affinoid cover
(U_{i})_{i\in I}
satisfying the conditions in
(i)
, and let
(V_{j})_{j\in J}
be an admissible affinoid cover of
X
such that
\mathscr{L}_{|V_{j}}
is a free
\mathcal{O}_{|V_{j}}
-module for each
j\in J
. As
X
is separated, for every
j\in J
we have an admissible affinoid cover
(U_{i}\cap V_{j})_{i\in I}
of
V_{j}
. Consider the following affinoid spaces:

W_{il}=(U_{i}\cap V_{j})\times(U_{l}\cap V_{k})=(U_{i}\times U_{l})\cap(V_{j}\times V_{k}).

For each
j,k\in J
, we have an admissible affinoid cover of
V_{j}\times V_{k}
given by
(W_{il})_{i,l\in I}
. By assumption, we have
\mathcal{M}_{|U_{i}\times U_{l}}\in\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|U_{i}\times U_{l}})
. Hence, by Corollary
6.2.13
, and Lemma
6.3.2
, it follows that
\mathcal{M}_{|W_{il}}\in\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|W_{il}})
. Again, using these two results, it follows that
\mathcal{M}_{|V_{j}\times V_{k}}\in\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|V_{j}\times V_{k}})
for each
j,k\in J
.
∎

Definition 6.3.4
.

Let
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
be the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
given by the modules satisfying the conditions of Lemma
6.3.3
. We call
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
the category of co-admissible modules over
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
.

We will now study the properties of
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
:

Lemma 6.3.5
.

\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
is an abelian category.

Proof.

As
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
is a quasi-abelian category, it suffices to show that every morphism
f:\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}
in
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
is strict. As this is a local property, we may assume that
X
is affinoid and
\mathscr{L}
is free. In this case, by Proposition
6.2.9

for any
V\in X^{2}
, the map
f:\mathcal{M}(V)\rightarrow\mathcal{N}(V)
is a morphism of co-admissible modules over a Fréchet-Stein algebra. This is strict by
[
42
, Corollary 3.4]
. Thus, the map
f:\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}
is strict, as we wanted.
∎

Theorem 6.3.6
.

There are mutually inverse equivalences of abelian categories:

\operatorname{S}:\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))\leftrightarrows\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})):\operatorname{S}^{-1}.

Furthermore, this equivalence preserves the supports of the sheaves.

Proof.

It suffices to show that
\operatorname{S}
and
\operatorname{S}^{-1}
send co-admissible modules to co-admissible modules. This may be done locally, so we may assume
X
is affinoid and
\mathscr{L}
is a free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module. But then this follows by Propositions
6.2.12
and
6.3.1
.
∎

We can finally give a global definition of
\mathcal{C}
-complexes:

Definition 6.3.7
.

A complex
C^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
is called a
\mathcal{C}
-complex if there is an affinoid cover
(U_{i})_{i\in I}
of
X
satisfying the following:

(i)

\mathscr{L}_{|U_{i}}
is a globally free
\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}}
-module for each
i\in I
.

(ii)

C_{|U_{i}\times U_{j}}^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})_{|U_{i}\times U_{j}})
for each
i,j\in I
.

We denote the full subcategory of all
\mathcal{C}
-complexes by
\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
.

Definition 6.3.8
.

Given a closed embedding
Y\rightarrow X^{2}
, we let
\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}^{Y}}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))
be the full subcategory of
\mathcal{C}
-complexes with cohomology supported on
Y
.

After all this preparation, we are finally ready to obtain a side-switching equivalence for
\mathcal{C}
-complexes:

Proposition 6.3.9
.

Let
X
be a smooth and separated rigid variety with a Lie algebroid
\mathscr{L}
. We have mutually inverse equivalences of triangulated categories:

\operatorname{S}:\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))\leftrightarrows\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})):\operatorname{S}^{-1}.

Furthermore, for any closed immersion
Y\rightarrow X
, these equivalences restrict to a pair of mutually inverse equivalences of triangulated categories:

\operatorname{S}:\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}^{Y}}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))\leftrightarrows\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}^{Y}}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})):\operatorname{S}^{-1}.

Proof.

This follows by Theorem
6.3.6
and Proposition
6.2.16
.
∎

As stated in Remark
6.2.4
, all the results thus far hold for the categories of right
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
-modules. In particular, we also have a category of right
\mathcal{C}
-complexes over
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
, and an equivalence of triangulated categories:

\operatorname{S}_{r}:\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})^{\operatorname{op}})\leftrightarrows\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}):\operatorname{S}_{r}^{-1}.

Thus, we have the following corollary:

Corollary 6.3.10
.

There is an equivalence of triangulated categories:

\operatorname{T}:\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))\rightarrow\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}),

which restricts to an equivalence of abelian categories:

\operatorname{T}:\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}).

Furthermore, if
Y\rightarrow X
is a closed immersion, then the restriction:

\operatorname{T}:\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}^{Y}}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L}))\rightarrow\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}^{Y}}(\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}),

is also an equivalence of triangulated categories.

Proof.

By Proposition
6.1.10
we have the identity:

\operatorname{T}=\operatorname{S}_{r}\left(\Omega_{\mathscr{L}^{2}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}}\operatorname{S}^{-1}(-)\right).

Thus, it suffices to show that each of the functors on the right hand side satisfies the statements of the corollary. This is a consequence of Proposition
6.3.9
, as well as
[
9
, Theorem 4.11]
.
∎

Remark 6.3.11
.

In virtue of this corollary, we will from now on work with left
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
-modules unless otherwise mentioned. In particular, the results will be stated and proved for left modules. The equivalence
\operatorname{T}
ensures that the corresponding facts for right modules still hold.

7.
Co-admissibility and the immersion functor

Let
X
be a separated smooth rigid analytic space with a Lie algebroid
\mathscr{L}
. This section is devoted to understanding the behavior of co-admissibility with respect to the immersion functor:

\Delta_{*}^{\operatorname{S}}:\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})^{e})\rightarrow\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2})).

In particular, our goal is studying certain subcategories of
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})^{e})

such that their essential images under
\Delta^{\operatorname{S}}_{*}
are Serre subcategories of
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}^{2}))
. This will allow us to employ the homological machinery of
\mathcal{C}
-complexes to our study of Hochschild (co)-homology. Due to some technical issues, we will focus on the case
\mathscr{L}=\mathcal{T}_{X/K}
. However, We would like to point out that we believe that most of the technology developed in this section should generalize to the case of an arbitrary Lie algebroid. For this reason, we will work in the general setting as much as we are currently able to.

The first step is identifying a notion of co-admissible
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}
-bimodules that behaves well with respect to the immersion functor. Recall that our interest of the bi-enveloping algebra
\wideparen{E}_{X}
stems from the fact that
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e}
is a very complicated sheaf. In particular, it is not clear if
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e}
is locally Fréchet-Stein. In fact, it is not even clear if
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e}
is a sheaf of complete bornological algebras. Thus, we cannot rely on the properties of
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e}
to define a good notion of co-admissibility for bimodules.

As a first approximation, we will define

\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{B}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
as the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e})
given by the modules satisfying that they are co-admissible as left and right
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}
-modules. This category satisfies some of the properties we are looking for. In particular, we will show below that if
X
is an affinoid space admitting an étale map
X\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{n}_{K}
, and
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{B}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
, then
\mathcal{M}(X)
is canonically a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}_{X}(X^{2})
-module. However, it is not clear that
\Delta_{*}(\mathcal{M})
is a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}_{X}
-module. Indeed, under the assumptions above,
\Delta_{*}(\mathcal{M})
is a co-admissible if and only if we have an isomorphism of
\wideparen{E}_{X}
-modules:

\Delta^{E}_{*}(\mathcal{M})=\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M}(X)),

and it is not clear that the right hand side has support contained in
\Delta\subset X^{2}
.

In order to remedy this, we need to impose conditions on
\mathcal{M}(X)
