which ensure that
\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M}(X))
has support contained in the diagonal. This line of thought leads to the category of co-admissible diagonal bimodules, which we denote

\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
. As we will see below,
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
is a full abelian subcategory of
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})

which satisfies that the immersion functor:

\Delta_{*}^{\operatorname{S}}:\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X^{2}})_{\Delta},

is an exact immersion of abelian categories. Furthermore, the essential image of
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
is a Serre subcategory of
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X^{2}})_{\Delta}
.

As above, these results are later generalized in the geometric and homological directions. In the geometric direction, we will show that the definitions can be generalized to an arbitrary separated and smooth rigid analytic space
X
. In the homological direction, we will define an adequate notion of diagonal
\mathcal{C}
-complexes, and show that the results above extend to the corresponding triangulated categories.

7.1.
Categories of co-admissible bimodules

Our study of Hochschild cohomology will be centered around the study of the following category:

Definition 7.1.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{B}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}))

be the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(U(\mathscr{L})^{e})
given by objects
\mathcal{M}
, such that
\mathcal{M}
is co-admissible as a
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})
-module and as a
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})^{\operatorname{op}}
-module. We call
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{B}(\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L}))
the category of co-admissible
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})
-bimodules.

In this section we will show that, locally on
X
, global sections of co-admissible bimodules are co-admissible over the complete bi-enveloping algebra of
\mathscr{L}
.

The contents of this section rely heavily on techniques from
p
-adic functional analysis. For this reason, it will be convenient to work with locally convex or bornological spaces instead of Ind-Banach spaces. We remark that all the spaces we will be interested in are (bornologifications of) Fréchet spaces which satisfy some nuclearity condition. Thus, not much will be lost with this change (
cf.

[
9
, Lemma 4.3]
).

We denote the category of locally convex
K
-vector spaces by
LCS_{K}
, and let
\widehat{\otimes}_{K}^{LCS}
be the completed projective tensor product in
LCS_{K}
(cf.
[
41
, Chapter IV 17.B]
). As before, we let
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}c_{K}
be the category of complete bornological spaces, and let
\widehat{\otimes}_{K}
denote the completed projective bornological tensor product.

Assume that
X=\operatorname{Sp}(A)
is a smooth affinoid variety, and let
\mathfrak{X}=\operatorname{Spf}(\mathcal{A})
be an affine formal model of
X
. Additionally, we will assume that
L:=\mathscr{L}(X)
admits a free
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{A})
-Lie lattice
\mathcal{L}
. As shown in Corollary
6.2.3
, for each non-negative integer
n,m\geq 0
we have a Fréchet-Stein presentation:

\wideparen{E}(L)=\varprojlim_{s}\left(\widehat{U}(\pi^{n+s}\mathcal{L})_{K}\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m+s}\mathcal{L})_{K}^{\operatorname{op}}\right)=\wideparen{U}(L)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}(L)^{\operatorname{op}}.

In order to keep thing simple, for each non-negative integer
n,m\geq 0
we will write:

\widehat{E}_{n,m}:=\widehat{U}(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L})_{K}\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\widehat{U}(\pi^{m}\mathcal{L})_{K}^{\operatorname{op}},\quad\widehat{U}_{n}=\widehat{U}(\pi^{n}\mathcal{L})_{K}.

Similarly, we write
\wideparen{E}=\wideparen{E}(L)
and
\wideparen{U}=\wideparen{U}(L)
. Let
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{U})
be a co-admissible
\wideparen{U}
-module. In particular, writing
\mathcal{M}_{n}:=\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\mathcal{M}
, we have:
\mathcal{M}=\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{M}_{n}
.

Definition 7.1.2
.

We define the following full subcategories of
\operatorname{Mod}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}c}(\wideparen{E})
:

(i)

Let
\mathcal{C}_{L}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{U})

be given by the objects
\mathcal{M}
which are co-admissible
\wideparen{U}
-modules.

(ii)

Let
\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{U})

be given by the objects
\mathcal{M}
which are co-admissible
\wideparen{U}^{\operatorname{op}}
-modules.

(iii)

Let
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{U})

be the intersection of the two previous categories.

Let
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{L}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{U}(L))
, and
\mathcal{M}=\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{M}_{n}
be a co-admissible
\wideparen{U}
-module presentation of
\mathcal{M}
. As shown in
[
3
, Section 7.2]
,
there is a canonical
\wideparen{U}
-linear isomorphism:

\operatorname{End}_{\wideparen{U}}(\mathcal{M})=\varprojlim_{n}\operatorname{End}_{\widehat{U}_{n}}(\mathcal{M}_{n}).

Each
\mathcal{M}_{n}
is a finite module over a Banach
K
-algebra. Hence, it is a
K
-Banach space. Thus, we may regard
\operatorname{End}_{K}(\mathcal{M}_{n})
as a Banach space with respect to the operator norm. The canonical inclusion
\operatorname{End}_{\widehat{U}_{n}}(\mathcal{M}_{n})\subset\operatorname{End}_{K}(\mathcal{M}_{n})
has closed image. Thus,
\operatorname{End}_{\widehat{U}_{n}}(\mathcal{M}_{n})
is also a Banach space.
Hence, we may regard
\operatorname{End}_{\wideparen{U}}(\mathcal{M})
as a topological
K
-vector space with respect to the inverse limit topology. This topology turns
\operatorname{End}_{\wideparen{U}}(\mathcal{M})
into a Fréchet space. From now on, we will always regard
\operatorname{End}_{\wideparen{U}}(\mathcal{M})
as a Fréchet space with respect to this topology.

By definition of
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{L}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{U}(L))
, we have a bounded action:

\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\left(\wideparen{U}\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}^{\operatorname{op}}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}.

Furthermore, by
[
9
, Proposition 5.5]
, the spaces

\mathcal{M}
,
\wideparen{U}
and
\wideparen{U}^{\operatorname{op}}
are
A
-nuclear Fréchet spaces. Hence, we may apply
[
9
, Corollary 5.20]
to show that this map arises from a continuous action:

\mathcal{M}\widehat{\otimes}^{LCS}_{K}\left(\wideparen{U}\widehat{\otimes}^{LCS}_{K}\wideparen{U}^{\operatorname{op}}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{M},

between the associated locally convex spaces. The properties of the projective tensor product in
LCS_{K}
show that this is equivalent to a continuous map:

\wideparen{U}^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow\operatorname{End}_{\wideparen{U}}(\mathcal{M}).

As both spaces are Fréchet, it follows that this map is continuous if and only if for every
n\geq 0
there is some
m\geq 0
such that we have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wideparen{U}^{\operatorname{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{End}_{\wideparen{U}}(\mathcal{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{End}_{\widehat{U}_{n}}(\mathcal{M}_{n})}

where all maps in the diagram are continuous maps of Banach
K
-algebras.

Proposition 7.1.3
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{L}(\wideparen{U})
, and assume that
\mathcal{M}
is co-admissible as an abstract
\wideparen{U}^{\operatorname{op}}
-module. Then the canonical topologies of
\mathcal{M}
as a co-admissible
\wideparen{U}
-module and as a co-admissible
\wideparen{U}^{\operatorname{op}}
-module agree. In particular,
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{B}(\wideparen{U})
.

Proof.

Define the following families of finite
\widehat{U}_{n}
-modules, resp. finite
\widehat{U}^{\operatorname{op}}_{m}
-modules:

\mathcal{M}_{n}=\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\mathcal{M},\quad\mathcal{M}^{r}_{m}=\widehat{U}^{\operatorname{op}}_{m}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}^{\operatorname{op}}}\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\widehat{U}_{m}.

Then we have the following inverse limits:

\mathcal{M}=\varprojlim\mathcal{M}_{n}\textnormal{, }\mathcal{M}=\varprojlim\mathcal{M}^{r}_{m},

where the first is an isomorphism of topological vector spaces, and the second one is an isomorphism of abstract
\wideparen{U}^{\operatorname{op}}
-modules. We let

\mathcal{M}^{r}
be
\mathcal{M}
regarded as a Fréchet space with respect to the topology given by the inverse limit
\mathcal{M}=\varprojlim\mathcal{M}^{r}_{m}
.

By the open mapping theorem,
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}^{r}
as topological
K
-vector spaces if and only if the identity map
\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{r}
is continuous. As shown above, the fact that
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{L}(\wideparen{U})
implies that the map
\Phi:\wideparen{U}^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow\operatorname{End}_{\wideparen{U}}(\mathcal{M})
is continuous. Hence, for each
n\geq 0
there is a minimal
m\geq 0
such that
\Phi
factors as a continuous map of Banach
K
-algebras:

\Phi_{n}:\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow\operatorname{End}_{\widehat{U}_{n}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{n}\right).

Fix
n,m\geq 0
as above. We have the following isomorphisms of
\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{K}\widehat{U}_{m}
-modules:

(39)

\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\mathcal{M}^{r}_{m}\rightarrow\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\widehat{U}_{m}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{n}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\widehat{U}_{m}.

Additionally, we have a pair of
\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}
-linear maps:

(40)

\mathcal{M}^{r}_{m}\rightarrow\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\mathcal{M}^{r}_{m},\quad\mathcal{M}_{n}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\widehat{U}_{m}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{n},

where the first one is the canonical inclusion
x\mapsto x\otimes 1
, and the second one is given by the
\wideparen{U}
-linear action of
\widehat{U}^{\operatorname{op}}_{m}
on
\mathcal{M}_{n}
induced by
\Phi_{n}
.

Combining the maps in
(\ref{equation 1 frechet topology coadmissible bimodules})
and
(\ref{equation 2 frechet topology coadmissible bimodules})
, we arrive at a
\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}
-linear map:

\psi_{n,m}:\mathcal{M}_{m}^{r}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{n},

which fits into the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{m}^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{n}}

where the upper horizontal map is the identity. As the topologies on
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}^{r}
are given by inverse limits, it is enough to show that
\psi_{n,m}
is continuous. However, as
\mathcal{M}_{m}^{r}
is a finite
\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}
-module, we can choose an epimorphism
\left(\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}\right)^{s}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{r}_{m}
, and it suffices to show continuity of the composition:

\left(\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}\right)^{s}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{r}_{m}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{n}.

Again, we may reduce it to showing that for any
x\in\mathcal{M}^{r}_{m}
, the map:

\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{n}\textnormal{, }f\mapsto xf,

is continuous. And this follows by
\Phi_{n}:\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow\operatorname{End}_{\widehat{U}_{n}}(\mathcal{M}_{n})
being continuous.
∎

We may use this to obtain a characterization of the elements of
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{B}(\wideparen{U})
:

Corollary 7.1.4
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}c_{K}}(\wideparen{E})
. The following are equivalent:

(i)

\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{L}(\wideparen{U})
and
\mathcal{M}
is co-admissible as an abstract
\wideparen{U}^{\operatorname{op}}
-module.

(ii)

\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{R}(\wideparen{U})
and
\mathcal{M}
is co-admissible as an abstract
\wideparen{U}
-module.

(iii)

\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{B}(\wideparen{U})
.

Our next goal is finding the relation between these categories and the category of co-admissible
\wideparen{E}
-modules. For the rest of the section, we fix some complete bornological module
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{L}(\wideparen{U})
. We will need the following technical lemmas:

Lemma 7.1.5
.

Let
B
be a Banach
K
-algebra, and
C\subset B
a closed
K
-subalgebra. Let
M
be a
B
-module which is finite as an
C
-module. Then the topologies of
M
as a finite
C
-module and as a finite
B
-module agree.

Proof.

Denote both topologies by
M^{C}
and
M^{B}
respectively. Let
m_{1},\cdots,m_{r}
be a finite system of generators of
M
as an
C
-module. Let
B^{\circ}
be the closed unit ball and
C^{\circ}=B^{\circ}\cap C
. Let
\mathcal{M}
be the
B^{\circ}
-module generated by
m_{1},\cdots,m_{r}
. We have a basis of open neighborhoods of zero in
M^{B}
given by the family
\left(\pi^{n}\mathcal{M}\right)_{n\geq 0}
. Notice that
\mathcal{M}
is an
C^{\circ}
-module which contains the
C^{\circ}
-module generated by
m_{1},\cdots,m_{r}
. Hence,
\mathcal{M}
is open in
M^{C}
. Thus, the identity map
M^{C}\rightarrow M^{B}
is
K
-linear, continuous and bijective. Therefore it is a homeomorphism by Banach’s open mapping theorem.
∎

Lemma 7.1.6
.

Let
R\rightarrow B,C\rightarrow D
be morphisms of
K
-algebras, and let
M
be an
R\otimes_{K}C
-module. There is a canonical
B\otimes_{K}D^{\operatorname{op}}
-linear isomorphism:

B\otimes_{K}D^{\operatorname{op}}\otimes_{R\otimes_{K}C^{\operatorname{op}}}M\rightarrow B\otimes_{R}M\otimes_{C}D.

Proof.

By Yoneda’s Lemma, it is enough to show that we have a natural isomorphism of functors:

\operatorname{Hom}_{B\otimes_{K}D^{\operatorname{op}}}(B\otimes_{R}M\otimes_{C}D,-)\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{B\otimes_{K}D^{\operatorname{op}}}(B\otimes_{K}D^{\operatorname{op}}\otimes_{R\otimes_{K}C^{\operatorname{op}}}M,-).

Let
N
be a
(B,D)
-bimodule and
\varphi:M\rightarrow N
be a morphism of
(R,C)
-bimodules. In particular,
\varphi
is a morphism of left
R
-modules. Hence, the extension-restriction adjunction associated to the map of
K
-algebras
R\rightarrow B
yields a unique map:

\displaystyle\varphi^{\prime}:B

\displaystyle\otimes_{R}M\rightarrow N

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle\otimes m\mapsto b\varphi(m)

Notice that
B\otimes_{R}M
is a right
C
-module, with action
(b\otimes m)a=b\otimes(ma)
. Furthermore, as
\varphi
is a morphism of
(R,C)
-bimodules, we have:

\varphi^{\prime}(b\otimes ma)=b\varphi(ma)=(b\varphi(m))a.

Applying the extension-restriction adjunction with respect to
C^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow D^{\operatorname{op}}
yields:

\varphi^{\prime\prime}:B\otimes_{R}M\otimes_{C}D\rightarrow N.

Hence, we can define a
K
-linear map:

\Psi:\operatorname{Hom}_{R\otimes C^{\operatorname{op}}}(M,N)\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{B\otimes D^{\operatorname{op}}}(B\otimes_{R}M\otimes_{C}D,N),\textnormal{ }\varphi\mapsto\varphi^{\prime\prime}.

Notice that the inclusion
i:M\rightarrow B\otimes_{R}M\otimes_{C}D
,
m\mapsto 1\otimes m\otimes 1
is
R\otimes_{K}C^{\operatorname{op}}
-linear. Hence, for any
B\otimes D^{\operatorname{op}}
-linear map
\psi:B\otimes_{R}M\otimes_{C}D\rightarrow N
we get an
R\otimes_{K}C^{\operatorname{op}}
-linear map
\psi\circ i
, by pullback. Hence, we get a
K
-linear map:

i^{*}:\operatorname{Hom}_{B\otimes_{K}D^{\operatorname{op}}}(B\otimes_{R}M\otimes_{C}D,N)\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{R\otimes_{K}C^{\operatorname{op}}}(M,N),\textnormal{ }\varphi\mapsto\varphi\circ i.

Showing that
\Psi
and
i^{*}
are mutually inverse is a straightforward calculation.
∎

For simplicity, we will write
V=\wideparen{U}\otimes_{K}\wideparen{U}^{\operatorname{op}}
until the end of this section.

Lemma 7.1.7
.

For any
n,m\geq 0
, consider the following
\widehat{E}_{n,m}
-module:

\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}=\widehat{E}_{n,m}\otimes_{V}M.

Then
\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}
is a finite
\widehat{E}_{n,m}
-module, and the map
\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}
has dense image.

Proof.

Fix non-negative integers
n,m\geq 0
. In order to show that
\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}
is a finite
\widehat{E}_{n,m}
-module, it is enough to show that:

(41)

\left(\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{K}\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}\right)\otimes_{V}\mathcal{M},

is a finite
\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{K}\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}
-module. By Lemma
7.1.6
, it follows that the module in
(\ref{equation 1 co-admissible over E(L)})
is canonically isomorphic to:

(42)

\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\widehat{U}_{m}.

By definition of co-admissible
\wideparen{U}
-module, we have a
\widehat{U}_{n}
-linear isomorphism:

\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{n}.

Hence, we have a
\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{K}\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}
-linear isomorphism:

(43)

\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\widehat{U}_{m}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{n}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\widehat{U}_{m}.

\mathcal{M}_{n}
is a finitely generated
\widehat{U}_{n}
-module by definition of co-admissibility. Hence, it follows that
\left(\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{K}\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}\right)\otimes_{V}\mathcal{M}
is a finite
\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{K}\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}
-module, as wanted.

In order to show that
\mathcal{M}
is dense in
\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}
, choose a simple tensor
a\otimes m\in\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}
. By Corollary
6.2.3
, we have:

\widehat{V}=\wideparen{U}\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\wideparen{U}^{\operatorname{op}}=\varprojlim_{s}\widehat{E}_{n+s,m+s}.

In particular,
\widehat{E}_{n,m}
is the completion of
V
with respect to some norm. Hence, we may choose a sequence
\left(a_{n}\right)_{n\geq 0}
in
V
which converges to
a
in
\widehat{E}_{n,m}
. As
\widehat{E}_{n,m}
is a two-sided noetherian Banach
K
-algebra, and
\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}
is finite module, we may regard
\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}
as a topological module. Furthermore, the action of
\widehat{E}_{n,m}
on
\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}
is separately continuous. Hence, we have:

a\otimes m=\left(\varinjlim a_{n}\right)\otimes m=\varinjlim\left(a_{n}\otimes m\right)=\varinjlim\left(1\otimes a_{n}m\right).

Thus, every tensor can be approximated by elements in the image of
\mathcal{M}
.
∎

Lemma 7.1.8
.

Let
n,m\geq 0
, and assume we have a continuous factorization:

\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow\operatorname{End}_{\widehat{U}_{n}}(\mathcal{M}_{n}).

Then there is a
\widehat{E}_{n,m}
-module structure on
\mathcal{M}_{n}
, and a
\widehat{E}_{n,m}
-linear isomorphism:

\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}:=\widehat{E}_{n,m}\otimes_{V}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{n}.

Proof.

Our assumptions imply that
\mathcal{M}_{n}
is a topological module over
\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{K}\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}
. As
\mathcal{M}_{n}
is a Banach
K
-vector space, it is complete. Hence, the action of
\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{K}\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}
on
\mathcal{M}_{n}
extends to an action of
\widehat{E}_{n,m}
. Furthermore, as
\mathcal{M}_{n}
is a finite
\widehat{U}_{n}
-module, it is also a finite
\widehat{E}_{n,m}
-module.

The projection
\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{n}
is
V
-linear. Thus, it induces a map of
\widehat{E}_{n,m}
-modules:

\psi:\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}:=\widehat{E}_{n,m}\otimes_{V}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{n}.

As the image of
\mathcal{M}
is dense in
\mathcal{M}_{n}
, it follows that the image of
\psi
is dense in
\mathcal{M}_{n}
. Since
\widehat{E}_{n,m}
is a noetherian Banach
K
-algebra, every submodule of a finitely generated module is closed. Hence, the image of
\psi
is both dense and closed, so
\psi
is surjective. The proof of Lemma
7.1.7
shows that:

\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}=\widehat{E}_{n,m}\otimes_{\left(\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{K}\widehat{U}_{m}^{\operatorname{op}}\right)}\left(\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\widehat{U}_{m}\right).

Thus, there is a bounded
K
-linear map
j:\mathcal{M}_{n}=\widehat{U}_{n}\otimes_{\wideparen{U}}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}
of the form:

j(a\otimes m)=1\otimes\left(a\otimes m\otimes 1\right).

In particular, we have
\psi\circ j=\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{M}_{n}}
, so we get a decomposition:

(44)

\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}=\operatorname{Ker}(\psi)\oplus\mathcal{M}_{n}.

Let
\overline{\mathcal{M}}
denote the image of
\mathcal{M}
inside
\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}
. Our construction of
j
shows that
\overline{\mathcal{M}}\subset\mathcal{M}_{n}
. However, by Lemma
7.1.7
,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}
is dense in
\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}
. As
\mathcal{M}_{n}
is closed in
\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}
, it follows that
\operatorname{Ker}(\psi)=0
.
∎

All these technical lemmas allow us to show the following proposition:

Proposition 7.1.9
.

There is a canonical isomorphism of
\wideparen{E}
-modules:

\mathcal{M}=\varprojlim_{n,m}\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}.

In particular,
\mathcal{M}
is a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}
-module

Proof.

By Lemma
7.1.7
, each of the

\widehat{E}_{n,m}
-modules
\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}
is finitely generated. Furthermore, by construction, we have:

\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}=\widehat{E}_{n,m}\otimes_{\widehat{E}_{n+1,m}}\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n+1,m},\quad\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}=\widehat{E}_{n,m}\otimes_{\widehat{E}_{n,m+1}}\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m+1}.

Hence, the
\wideparen{E}
-module
\varprojlim_{n,m}\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}

is a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}
-module. We just need to show that this inverse limit is isomorphic to
\mathcal{M}
as an
\wideparen{E}
-module.

As
\mathcal{M}
is a co-admissible
\wideparen{U}
-bimodule, the discussion at the beginning of the section shows that for each
n\geq 0
there is a minimal
m(n)\geq 0
such that we have a continuous factorization:

\widehat{U}_{m(n)}^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow\operatorname{End}_{\widehat{U}_{n}}(\mathcal{M}_{n}).

Hence, by Lemma
7.1.8
we have an isomorphism
\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m(n)}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{n}
. As the family
\left(\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m(n)}\right)_{n\geq 0}
is co-initial in
\left(\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}\right)_{n,m\geq 0}
, it follows that we have:

\varprojlim_{n,m}\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m}=\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m(n)}=\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{M}_{n}=\mathcal{M}.

Thus showing that
\mathcal{M}
is a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}
-module.
∎

Corollary 7.1.10
.

The topologies of
\mathcal{M}
as a co-admissible
\wideparen{U}
-module and as a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}
-module agree.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.1.8
, for each
n\geq 0
, there is some
m(n)\geq 0
such that
\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m(n)}=\mathcal{M}_{n}
as
\widehat{E}_{n,m(n)}
-modules. By Lemma
7.1.5
, the topologies on
\mathcal{M}_{n}
induced by
\widehat{U}_{n}
and
\widehat{E}_{n,m(n)}
agree. By Proposition
7.1.9
, we have:
\mathcal{M}=\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{M}^{b}_{n,m(n)}
. As the topologies of
\mathcal{M}
as a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}
-module and
\wideparen{U}
-module are determined by this inverse limit, it follows that they agree.
∎

We may condense the contents of this section into the following theorem:

Theorem 7.1.11
.

There is an embedding of abelian categories:

\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{L}(\wideparen{U})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}),

which satisfies the following properties:

(i)

For
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{L}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{U})
, the canonical topologies of
\mathcal{M}
as a co-admissible
\wideparen{U}
-module and as a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}
-module agree.

(ii)

\mathcal{C}_{L}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{U})
is a Serre subcategory of
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E})
.

The corresponding results for
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{R}(\wideparen{U}(L))
hold mutatis-mutandis.

Proof.

As item
(i)
is Corollary
7.1.10
, we only need to show item
(ii)
. Consider the following short exact sequence of co-admissible
\wideparen{E}
-modules:

0\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{3}\rightarrow 0.

As morphisms of co-admissible modules are strict, it follows that
\mathcal{M}_{1}
is a closed submodule of
\mathcal{M}_{2}
, and that it has the subspace topology. Similarly,
\mathcal{M}_{3}
carries the quotient topology.

Assume
\mathcal{M}_{2}
is in
\mathcal{C}_{L}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{U})
. By item
(i)
and the discussion above,
\mathcal{M}_{1}
is a closed
\wideparen{U}
-submodule of
\mathcal{M}_{2}
with respect to its topology as a co-admissible
\wideparen{U}
-module. Hence,
by
[
42
, Lemma 3.6]
, it follows that
\mathcal{M}_{1}
and
\mathcal{M}_{3}
are co-admissible
\wideparen{U}
-modules.

Conversely, assume that
\mathcal{M}_{1}
and
\mathcal{M}_{3}
are objects of
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{L}(\wideparen{U})
. By
[
42
, Remark 3.2]
, the map
\wideparen{U}\rightarrow\widehat{U}_{n}
is two- sided flat for all
n\geq 0
. Hence, tensoring by
\widehat{U}_{n}
we obtain a family of short exact sequences of
\widehat{U}_{n}
-modules:

0\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{1,n}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{2,n}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{3,n}\rightarrow 0,

where
\mathcal{M}_{1,n}
and
\mathcal{M}_{3,n}
are finite
\widehat{U}_{n}
-modules by assumption. As
\widehat{U}_{n}
is two-sided noetherian, it follows that
\mathcal{M}_{2,n}
is also finite. Taking inverse limits, and using the fact that inverse limits associated to coherent sheaves of
\wideparen{U}
-modules satisfy the Mittag-Leffler condition, it follows that we have a short exact sequence:

0\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{1}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{M}_{2,n}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{3}\rightarrow 0.

By the Snake Lemma, it follows that we have a
\wideparen{U}
-linear isomorphism:

\psi:\mathcal{M}_{2}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{M}_{2,n}.

This is a continuous isomorphism, as continuity of the map
\wideparen{U}\rightarrow\widehat{U}_{n}
implies that the maps
\mathcal{M}_{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{2,n}
are also continuous. Thus,
\mathcal{M}_{2}
is a co-admissible
\wideparen{U}
-module, and hence an object of
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{L}(\wideparen{U})
.
∎

7.2.
Co-admissibility and the immersion functor I

Now we will make use of the results in the preceding section to construct sheaves of co-admissible
\wideparen{E}(\mathscr{L})
-modules with support contained in the diagonal
\Delta\subset X^{2}
. As we will make extensive use of Kashiwara’s equivalence (cf.
[
4
, Theorem 7.1]
,
[
9
, Proposition 9.5]
), we will focus on the case
\mathscr{L}=\mathcal{T}_{X/K}
. In this case we have
\mathcal{T}_{X/K}^{2}=\mathcal{T}_{X^{2}/K}
, so that we have:

\wideparen{U}(\mathcal{T}_{X/K}^{2})=\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X^{2}},\quad\wideparen{E}_{X}:=\wideparen{E}(\mathcal{T}_{X/K}).

As usual, we deal with the local case first. Let
X=\operatorname{Sp}(A)
be a smooth affinoid variety equipped with an étale map
X\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{n}
. In particular, there are some rigid functions
f_{1},\cdots,f_{n}\in A
such that
df_{1},\cdots,df_{n}
are an
A
-basis of
\Omega_{X}(X)
. Hence, there is a basis
v_{1},\cdots,v_{n}
of
\mathcal{T}_{X/K}
satisfying
df_{i}(v_{j})=\delta_{i,j}
, for
1\leq i,j\leq n
.
Notice that in this case,
\mathcal{T}_{X/K}
satisfies the conditions of Section
7.1
.

Let
\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}
be the coherent sheaf of ideals of
\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}
associated to the diagonal embedding
\Delta:X\rightarrow X^{2}
. By abuse of notation, we will often times identify
\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}
with its global sections, and simply write
\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}=\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}(X^{2})
when no confusion is possible. We now introduce some machinery from
[
4
]
:

Definition 7.2.1

(
[
4
, Definition 5.2]
)
.

Let
I
be an ideal in a commutative
K
-algebra
B
and let
L
be a
(K,B)
-Lie algebra. We call a subset
\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\}\subset L
an
I
-standard basis if the following hold:

(i)

\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\}
is a
B
-module basis of
L

(ii)

There is a generating set
\{g_{1},\cdots,g_{r}\}
of
I
with
r\leq n
.

(iii)

x_{i}\cdot g_{j}=\delta_{i,j}
for
1\leq i\leq n
, and
1\leq j\leq r
.

We can now adapt this concept to our case of interest:

Lemma 7.2.2
.

Consider the following ideals for
1\leq r\leq n
:

\mathcal{J}_{r}=\left(1\otimes f_{1}-f_{1}\otimes 1,\cdots,1\otimes f_{r}-f_{r}\otimes 1\right),

and let
\mathcal{J}=\mathcal{J}_{n}
. The following hold:

(i)

The following subset is a
\mathcal{J}
-standard basis:

\mathcal{V}:=\{1\otimes v_{1},\cdots,1\otimes v_{n},1\otimes v_{1}+v_{1}\otimes 1,\cdots,1\otimes v_{n}+v_{n}\otimes 1\}\subset\mathcal{T}_{X^{2}/K}(X).

(ii)

Let
B_{r}=A\widehat{\otimes}_{K}A/\mathcal{J}_{r}
. Then
Y_{r}=\operatorname{Sp}(B_{r})
is a smooth affinoid space, and we write
Y=Y_{n}
.

(iii)

We have a closed immersion
i:X\rightarrow Y
making
X
a union of connected components of
Y
.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.1.4
, we know that the choice of an
A
-basis
v_{1},\cdots,v_{n}
of
\mathcal{T}_{X/K}(X)
induces a basis
1\otimes v_{1},\cdots,1\otimes v_{n},v_{1}\otimes 1,\cdots,v_{n}\otimes 1
of
\mathcal{T}_{X^{2}/K}(X^{2})
. A simple calculation then shows that
\mathcal{V}
is also an
A\widehat{\otimes}_{K}A
-basis of
\mathcal{T}_{X^{2}/K}(X^{2})
. Furthermore, for
1\leq i\leq n
, and
1\leq j\leq n
, we have:

1\otimes v_{i}(1\otimes f_{j}-f_{j}\otimes 1)=\delta_{i,j},\textnormal{ and }(1\otimes v_{i}+v_{i}\otimes 1)(1\otimes f_{j}-f_{j}\otimes 1)=0.

Thus,
\mathcal{V}
is a
\mathcal{J}
-adapted basis of
\mathcal{T}_{X^{2}/K}(X^{2})
, and
(i)
holds.

For statement
(ii)
, fix
1\leq r\leq n
, and consider the standard right-exact sequence of coherent
B
-modules associated to the closed immersion
Y_{r}\rightarrow X^{2}
:

(45)

\mathcal{J}_{r}/\mathcal{J}_{r}^{2}\rightarrow B_{r}\otimes_{A\widehat{\otimes}_{K}A}\Omega_{X^{2}/K}(X^{2})\rightarrow\Omega_{Y_{r}/K}(Y_{r})\rightarrow 0.

In order to show that
Y_{r}
is smooth, it suffices to show that
(\ref{equation showing that A^2/J is smooth})
is split and exact. By definition, the image in
B_{r}\otimes_{A\widehat{\otimes}_{K}A}\Omega_{X^{2}/K}(X^{2})
of the equivalence class in
\mathcal{J}_{r}/\mathcal{J}_{r}^{2}
of each of the generators
1\otimes f_{j}-f_{j}\otimes 1
is given by the expression:

1\otimes\left(1\otimes d(f_{j})-d(f_{j})\otimes 1\right).

Applying
5.1.4
again, it follows that
\Omega_{X^{2}/K}(X^{2})
has a
A\widehat{\otimes}_{K}A
-basis given by elements of the form
1\otimes d(f_{j}),d(f_{j})\otimes 1
for
1\leq j\leq n
. Thus, applying the same argument as above, it follows that the elements of the form:

1\otimes d(f_{j})-d(f_{j})\otimes 1,1\otimes d(f_{j})\textnormal{, where }1\leq j\leq n,

also form a basis of
\Omega_{X^{2}/K}(X^{2})
. Thus, we obtain a split of the sequence
(\ref{equation showing that A^2/J is smooth})
by defining a map:

B_{r}\otimes_{A\widehat{\otimes}_{K}A}\Omega_{X^{2}/K}(X^{2})\rightarrow\mathcal{J}_{r}/\mathcal{J}_{r}^{2},\quad 1\otimes\left(1\otimes d(f_{j})-d(f_{j})\otimes 1\right)\mapsto 1\otimes f_{j}-f_{j}\otimes 1,

for
1\leq j\leq r
, and sending the rest of the basis to
0
. Therefore
Y_{r}
is smooth for each
1\leq r\leq n
.

In order to show
(iii)
, let
\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}
be the sheaf of ideals associated to the diagonal
\Delta:X\rightarrow X^{2}
. We notice that
\mathcal{J}\subset\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}
, so we have a closed immersion
X\rightarrow Y
. As
Y
is smooth, its dimension agrees with the rank of the vector bundle
\Omega_{Y/K}
. By our discussion above,
\mathcal{J}/\mathcal{J}^{2}
is a vector bundle of rank
n
, and by smoothness of
X^{2}
,
\Omega_{X^{2}/K}
is a vector bundle of rank
2n
. Thus, the fact that
(\ref{equation showing that A^2/J is smooth})
is exact implies that
\operatorname{dim}(Y)=n
. As
\operatorname{dim}(X)=n
, and
X
is smooth (in particular reduced), it follows that
X
is a union of irreducible components of
Y
. However, as
Y
is smooth, its irreducible components agree with its connected components, and we are done.
∎

Let us now introduce the following objects:

Definition 7.2.3

(
[
4
, Definitions 4.2 and 5.5]
)
.

Let
C
be an affinoid algebra,
\mathcal{M}
a complete bornological
C
-module and
\mathcal{I}\subset C
an ideal. Consider the following objects:

(i)

\mathcal{M}[\mathcal{I}]=\{m\in\mathcal{M}\textnormal{ }|\textnormal{ }cm=0\textnormal{ for all }c\in\mathcal{I}\}
.

(ii)

\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(\mathcal{I})=\{m\in\mathcal{M}\textnormal{ }|\textnormal{ }\varinjlim_{n}c^{n}m=0\textnormal{ for all }c\in\mathcal{I}\}
.

The relevance of the previous spaces is highlighted by the following theorem:

Theorem 7.2.4

(
[
4
, Theorem 6.7]
)
.

Let
X=\operatorname{Sp}(A)
be a smooth affinoid space with a Lie algebroid
\mathscr{L}
. Let
\mathcal{I}\subset\mathcal{O}
be the ideal associated to a closed immersion
Y\rightarrow X
. Assume
\mathscr{L}(X)
has an
\mathcal{I}(X)
-standard basis, and let
\mathcal{M}
be a co-admissible
\wideparen{U}(\mathscr{L})
-module. The following are equivalent:

(i)

\mathcal{M}
is supported on
Y
.

(ii)

\mathcal{M}(X)=\mathcal{M}(X)_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}(X))
.

As shown in the previous section, any bornological module
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{L}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X))
is canonically a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}_{X}(X^{2})
-module. Hence, by Definition
6.2.10
, we may consider the co-admissible
\wideparen{E}_{X}
-module
\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M})
on
X^{2}
. We are interested in finding conditions under which
\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M})
has support contained in the diagonal. As shown in Proposition
6.3.1
, the side-changing operators
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{*}
, and
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}
do not change the support, and send co-admissible modules to co-admissible modules. Hence,
\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M})
is supported on the diagonal if and only if
\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}(\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M}))
is a co-admissible
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X^{2}}
-module supported on the diagonal. Furthermore, the isomorphism:

\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}:\wideparen{E}_{X}\rightarrow\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X^{2}}

is the identity on
\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}
. In particular, we have the following identifications:

\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M})=\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M})=\operatorname{Loc}(\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}\mathcal{M}),

as sheaves of Ind-Banach
\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}
-modules.

Proposition 7.2.5
.

Consider a module
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}_{X}(X^{2}))
. Then the co-admissible
\wideparen{E}_{X}
-module
\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M})
is supported on the diagonal
\Delta\subset X^{2}
if and only if:

\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}).

Proof.

For simplicity of the notation, we will write
\mathcal{N}=\wideparen{\mathbb{T}}^{-1,*}\mathcal{M}
.
Assume that
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta})
. As discussed above, we have
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{N}
as complete bornological
A\widehat{\otimes}_{K}A
-modules. Hence, this implies

\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta})
. As
\mathcal{J}\subset\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}
, it follows that
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{\infty}(\mathcal{J})
. Furthermore, as shown in Lemma
7.2.2
,
\mathcal{T}_{X^{2}/K}(X^{2})
has a
\mathcal{J}
-adapted basis. Thus, Theorem
7.2.4
implies that
\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N})
has support contained in the closed sub variety
j:Y\rightarrow X^{2}
. By Kashiwara’s equivalence
[
4
, Theorem 7.1]
,
we have:

\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N})\simeq j_{+}j^{!}\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N}).

On the other hand,
j^{!}\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N})
is the co-admissible
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{Y}
-module with global sections:

\Gamma(Y,j^{!}\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N}))=\mathcal{N}[\mathcal{J}].

By statement
(iii)
in Lemma
7.2.2
,
X
is a union of connected components of
Y
. Hence, there is an element
f\in\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}
such that its image
\overline{f}\in A\widehat{\otimes}_{K}A/\mathcal{J}
satisfies:

Y(1/\overline{f})=Y\setminus X,\quad X=\mathbb{V}(\overline{f}).

Let
(f)
, and
(\overline{f})
be the ideals in
A\widehat{\otimes}_{K}A
and
A\widehat{\otimes}_{K}A/\mathcal{J}
generated by
f
and
\overline{f}
respectively. Then the following holds:

\mathcal{N}[\mathcal{J}]\subset\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}).

Thus, as
f\in\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}
, it follows that we have the following identifications:

\mathcal{N}[\mathcal{J}]=\mathcal{N}[\mathcal{J}]_{\infty}((f))=\mathcal{N}[\mathcal{J}]_{\infty}((\overline{f})).

By
[
4
, Corollary 6.6]
, it follows that
\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N}[\mathcal{J}])(Y(\frac{1}{\overline{f}}))=0
. Thus, the support of
\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N}[\mathcal{J}])
is contained in
X
. Let
i:X\rightarrow Y
denote the closed immersion. We may then apply Kashiwara’s equivalence again to obtain
\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N}[\mathcal{J}])\simeq i_{+}i^{!}\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N}[\mathcal{J}])
. Hence, we have:

\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N})\simeq j_{+}i_{+}i^{!}\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N}[\mathcal{J}])\simeq\Delta_{+}i^{!}\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N}[\mathcal{J}]),

where we are using the fact that
\Delta=j\circ i
, together with
[
9
, Lemma 7.9]
. Thus,
\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M})
has support contained in the diagonal, as we wanted to show.

Conversely, assume that
\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M})
has support contained in the diagonal. As the side-switching operators do not change the support, it follows that
\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N})
also has support contained in the diagonal. By
[
4
, Corollary 5.6]
, the submodule
\mathcal{N}_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta})\subset\mathcal{N}
is also co-admissible, and has support contained in the diagonal. Hence, by Kashiwara’s equivalence, we have:

\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N}_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}))\simeq\Delta_{+}\Delta^{!}\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N}_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}))\simeq\Delta_{+}\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N}_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta})[\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}])\\
=\Delta_{+}\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N}[\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}])\simeq\Delta_{+}\Delta^{!}\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N})\simeq\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{N}),

thus showing that
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta})
, as wanted. The converse follows at once from Kashiwara’s equivalence.
∎

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}_{X}
-module supported on the diagonal. By the side-switching for bimodules in Proposition
6.2.12
, together with Kashiwara’s equivalence, we have that:

\mathcal{M}\simeq\operatorname{S}\Delta_{+}\Delta^{!}\operatorname{S}^{-1}\mathcal{M}=\operatorname{S}\left(\Delta_{*}\left(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X^{2}\leftarrow X}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}}\Delta^{-1}\left(\operatorname{S}^{-1}\mathcal{M}\right)[\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}]\right)\right).

This will be key in our calculations with Hochschild (co)-homology bellow.

Lemma 7.2.6
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}_{X}
-module supported on the diagonal, and
V\subset X
be an affinoid subdomain. Then we have
\Delta^{-1}\mathcal{M}(V)=\mathcal{M}(V^{2})
.

Proof.

As
\mathcal{M}
has support contained in the diagonal, there is some sheaf of Ind-Banach spaces
\mathcal{N}
on
X
such that
\mathcal{M}=\Delta_{*}\mathcal{N}
. Thus, we have:

\mathcal{M}(V^{2})=\Delta_{*}\mathcal{N}(V^{2})=\mathcal{N}(V)=\Delta^{-1}\Delta_{*}\mathcal{N}(V)=\Delta^{-1}\mathcal{M}(V),

as we wanted to show.
∎

Recall the category of co-admissible bimodules
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
from Definition
7.1.1
.

Definition 7.2.7
.

We define
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
as the full subcategory of
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
given by objects
\mathcal{M}
satisfying that:

\mathcal{M}(X)=\mathcal{M}(X)_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}).

Expanding on our previous results, we wish to investigate the relation between
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
and
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}_{X})
. In particular, we want to determine the image of
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
under the extension functor:

\Delta_{*}^{E}:\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e})\rightarrow\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{E}_{X}).

Let us start with the following lemma:

Lemma 7.2.8
.

\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
is an abelian category.

Proof.

As
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
is a full subcategory of
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e})
, and this category is quasi-abelian, it suffices to show that it contains the kernels and cokernels of its maps, and that every morphism is strict. As every morphism between co-admissible modules is strict, it is clear that every morphism in
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
is strict. Let
f:\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}
be a morphism in
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
, and consider the strict exact complex:

(46)

0\rightarrow\operatorname{Ker}(f)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\operatorname{Coker}(f)\rightarrow 0.

As
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
and
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}^{\operatorname{op}}_{X})
are abelian categories, it is clear that:

\operatorname{Ker}(f),\operatorname{Coker}(f)\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}).

Furthermore, the fact that
X
is affinoid implies that
(\ref{equation bimodules supported on diagonal is abelian})
is exact after applying
\Gamma(X,-)
. In particular, we have a strict injection
\operatorname{Ker}(f)(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(X)
, and a strict surjection
\mathcal{N}(X)\rightarrow\operatorname{Coker}(f)(X)
. By assumption, we have
\mathcal{M}(X)=\mathcal{M}(X)_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta})
, and
\mathcal{N}(X)=\mathcal{N}(X)_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta})
. Thus, we have
\operatorname{Ker}(f)(X)=\operatorname{Coker}(f)(X)_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta})
and
\operatorname{Ker}(f)(X)=\operatorname{Coker}(f)(X)_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta})
, as we wanted to show.
∎

Proposition 7.2.9
.

We have an exact embedding of abelian categories:

\Delta_{*}^{E}:\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}_{X})_{\Delta}.

In particular, for
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
we have
\Delta^{E}_{*}\mathcal{M}=\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M}(X))
.

Proof.

Choose any
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
. By definition, we have
\mathcal{M}(X)\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X))
. Hence, it follows by Theorem
7.1.11

that
\mathcal{M}(X)\in\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}_{X}(X^{2}))
. Thus, we may consider the co-admissible
\wideparen{E}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{N}=\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M}(X))
. As
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
, it follows by Proposition
7.2.5
that
\mathcal{N}
is a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}_{X}
-module supported on
\Delta\subset X^{2}
. Consider the following presheaf in
X^{2}
:

\tilde{\mathcal{M}}:=\wideparen{E}_{X}\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\operatorname{psh}}_{p_{1}^{-1}\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}^{\operatorname{psh}}p_{2}^{-1}\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{\operatorname{op}}}\Delta_{*}\mathcal{M},

where the operator
\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\operatorname{\operatorname{psh}}}
is the tensor product in the category of presheaves of Ind-Banach spaces, and all the pullbacks are also taken in the category of presheaves.

With this definition, it follows that we have:

\Gamma(X^{2},\tilde{\mathcal{M}})=\mathcal{M}(X).

Thus, we may follow the arguments in the proof of Proposition
6.2.9
to show that
\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{M}(X)}
induces a unique
\wideparen{E}_{X}
-linear map of presheaves of Ind-Banach modules:

\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{M}}.

Consider the following morphism of presheaves:

\varphi:\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\rightarrow\Delta_{*}\Delta^{-1}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}=\Delta_{*}\left(\Delta^{-1}\wideparen{E}_{X}\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\operatorname{psh}}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e}}\mathcal{M}\right).

Notice that
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
has support contained in
\Delta
. Hence, the map
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}\rightarrow\Delta_{*}\Delta^{-1}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
induces an isomorphism on sheafifications. As the sheafification of
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
is
\Delta_{*}^{E}\mathcal{M}
, it is enough to show that
\varphi
induces an isomorphism:

\Delta^{-1}\mathcal{N}\rightarrow\Delta^{-1}\wideparen{E}_{X}\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\operatorname{psh}}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e}}\mathcal{M}=\Delta^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\operatorname{psh}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}\overrightarrow{\otimes}^{\operatorname{psh}}_{K}\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{M}=:\mathcal{H}.

Choose an affinoid subdomain
V\subset X
. By Lemma
7.2.6
we have the following:

(47)

\displaystyle\Delta^{-1}\mathcal{N}(V)=\mathcal{N}(V^{2})=

\displaystyle\wideparen{E}_{X}(V^{2})\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{E}_{X}(X^{2})}\mathcal{M}(X)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(V)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X)}\mathcal{M}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X)}\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(V),

where the last identity follows by the definition of
\wideparen{E}_{X}
, and Proposition
5.3.7
. By assumption,
\mathcal{M}
is a co-admissible module over
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}
and
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{\operatorname{op}}
. Thus, by definition of co-admissibility we have identities:

\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(V)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X)}\mathcal{M}(X)=\mathcal{M}(V)=\mathcal{M}(X)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X)}\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(V).

Hence, the identity (
47
) may be reformulated into the following chain of identities:

\displaystyle\Delta^{-1}\mathcal{N}(V)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(V)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X)}\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(V)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{M}(X)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X)}\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(V)\right)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X)}\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(V)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(X)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X)}\left(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(V)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X)}\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(V)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(X)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X)}\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(V)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(V).

On the other hand, by definition of the pullback in the category of presheaves:

\mathcal{H}(V)=\varinjlim_{U}\left(\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}(U)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}(V^{2})}\mathcal{M}(V)\right).

where the
U
range over all the affinoid subdomains
U\subset V^{2}
such that
V=\Delta(X)\cap U
. Hence, it is enough to show that we have an isomorphism:

\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}(U)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}(V^{2})}\mathcal{M}(V)=\mathcal{M}(V),

for each
U
as above. However, by definition we have that
\mathcal{N}
is a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}_{X}
-module with support contained in the diagonal. Hence, as
V^{2}\cap\Delta(X)=U\cap\Delta(X)
by our choice of
U
, it follows that we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(V)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}(V^{2})=\mathcal{N}(U)=\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}(U)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}(V^{2})}\mathcal{M}(V),

where the last identity follows by the definition of the
\operatorname{Loc}(-)
functor (
cf.
Proposition
6.2.9
), and the fact that in the current conditions there is an isomorphism
\wideparen{E}_{X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X^{2}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}K\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\}
. Hence, we have obtained an isomorphism:

\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M}(X))\rightarrow\Delta^{E}_{*}\mathcal{M},

as we wanted to show. This shows that the essential image of
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
under the extension functor
\Delta_{*}^{E}
is indeed contained in
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}_{X})_{\Delta}
. Furthermore, as
X
is affinoid with free tangent sheaf, exactness can be checked at the global sections, and thus it follows that
\Delta_{*}^{E}
is exact. Similarly, as morphisms in both categories are determined at the level of global sections, it follows that the functor is also fully faithful.
∎

We can draw a plethora of conclusions from this proposition:

Corollary 7.2.10
.

The immersion functor induces an exact embedding:

\Delta_{*}^{\operatorname{S}}:\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X^{2}})_{\Delta}.

Corollary 7.2.11
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e})
. The following are equivalent:

(i)

\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
.

(ii)

There is an affinoid cover
(V_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
of
X
such that
\mathcal{M}_{|V_{i}}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{V_{i}})_{\Delta}
.

(iii)

For any affinoid cover
(V_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
of
X
we have
\mathcal{M}_{|V_{i}}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{V_{i}})_{\Delta}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
, and let
(V_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
be an affinoid cover of
X
. By Kiehl’s Theorem for co-admissible modules, it follows that
\mathcal{M}_{|V_{i}}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{V_{i}})
for
1\leq i\leq n
. Thus, we only need to show that:

\mathcal{M}(V_{i})=\mathcal{M}(V_{i})_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}(V_{i})).

By Proposition
7.2.9
we have
\Delta^{E}_{*}\mathcal{M}=\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M}(X))
. Thus, for
1\leq i\leq n
we have:

(\Delta_{|V_{i}})^{E}_{*}\left(\mathcal{M}_{|V_{i}}\right)=\left(\Delta^{E}_{*}\mathcal{M}\right)_{|V_{i}^{2}}=\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M}(X))_{|V_{i}^{2}}=\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M}(V_{i})).

Thus, the co-admissible
\wideparen{E}_{V_{i}}
-module associated to
\mathcal{M}(V_{i})
has support contained in the diagonal. Hence, by Proposition
7.2.5
, we have
\mathcal{M}(V_{i})=\mathcal{M}(V_{i})_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}(V_{i}))
.
Thus,
(i)
implies
(ii)
and
(iii)
. The converse is clear.
∎

As a consequence of our results thus far, it follows that the category of co-admissible diagonal bimodules
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
presents similar behavior to
the usual category of co-admissible modules
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
. In our current setting, the sheaves in
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
are completely determined by their global sections, so one may expect that this phenomena carries over to
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
. We conclude this section by confirming this intuition. Let us start by defining the relevant categories:

Definition 7.2.12
.

Let
*=L,R
, or
B
. We define
\mathcal{C}_{*}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X))_{\Delta}
as the full subcategory of
\mathcal{C}_{*}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X))
, given by objects
\mathcal{M}
such that
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta})
.

Corollary 7.2.13
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{*}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X))
. Then
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}_{X}(X^{2}))
. Furthermore,
\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M})
is supported on
\Delta\subset X^{2}
if and only if
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{*}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X))_{\Delta}
.

Proof.

This is a consequence of Theorem
7.1.11
, and Proposition
7.2.5
.
∎

Our next goal is constructing a localization functor:

\operatorname{Loc}(-):\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X))_{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta},

which shows that co-admissible bimodules supported on the diagonal are completely determined by their global sections.

Proposition 7.2.14
.

There is an equivalence of abelian categories:

\operatorname{Loc}(-):\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X))_{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta},

given by sending
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X))_{\Delta}
to the unique
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e}
-module defined on affinoid subdomains
V\subset X
by:

\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M})(V)=\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(V)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X)}\mathcal{M}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X)}\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(V).

Proof.

Consider a module
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X))_{\Delta}
. Let
\mathcal{M}^{L}=\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M})
be the associated sheaf of co-admissible
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}
-modules,
\mathcal{M}^{R}=\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M})
be the analogous sheaf of co-admissible
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{\operatorname{op}}
-modules, and
\mathcal{M}^{B}:=\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M})
be the corresponding co-admissible
\wideparen{E}_{X}
-module. By Corollary
7.2.13
,
\mathcal{M}^{B}
is supported on the diagonal. Hence, for any affinoid subdomain
V\subset X
we have:

\mathcal{M}^{B}(V\times X)=\mathcal{M}^{B}(V\times V)=\mathcal{M}^{B}(X\times V).

Thus, we have the following identities of complete bornological
\wideparen{E}_{X}(V\times V)
-modules:

\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(V)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X)}\mathcal{M}=\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(V)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X)}\mathcal{M}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X)}\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(V)=\mathcal{M}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X)}\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(V).

In particular,
\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M})=\mathcal{M}^{L}=\mathcal{M}^{R}
. Hence, it follows that
\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M})\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
. The fact that this is an equivalence follows by Proposition
7.2.9
.
∎

Corollary 7.2.15
.

We have the following commutative diagram of categories:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X))_{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}_{X}(X^{2}))_{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}_{X}))_{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Loc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Loc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{*}}

satisfying the following properties:

(i)

The upper horizontal map is the restriction to
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(X))_{\Delta}
of the map constructed in Theorem
7.1.11
.

(ii)

The vertical maps are equivalences of abelian categories.

(iii)

The horizontal maps are embeddings of abelian categories. Furthermore, their essential images are Serre subcategories.

Proof.

This is a summary of the contents of previous sections. Namely, of the diagram follows by Propositions
7.2.9
and
7.2.14
. Claim
(ii)
is showed in Proposition
7.2.14
and Definition
6.2.10
, and
(iii)
was part of Theorem
7.1.11
.
∎

7.3.
Co-admissibility and the immersion functor II

In this section, we generalize the results of the previous sections to the non-affinoid setting. As before, Kashiwara’s equivalence will play an important role. Thus, we will only deal with the case
\mathscr{L}=\mathcal{T}_{X/K}
. For the rest of this section, we fix a separated smooth rigid analytic space
X
. Let us start with the following definition:

Definition 7.3.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
be the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e})
given by the modules
\mathcal{M}
such that there is an admissible affinoid cover
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
of
X
satisfying the following:

(i)

For each
i\in I
there is an étale map
V_{i}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{n}_{K}
.

(ii)

For each
i\in I
, we have
\mathcal{M}_{|V_{i}}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{V_{i}})_{\Delta}
.

We call
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
the category of co-admissible diagonal
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}
-bimodules.

Notice that if
X
is affinoid and satisfies that there is an étale map
X\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{n}_{K}
, then by Corollary
7.2.11
this definition agrees with the one given in Definition
7.2.7
. Furthermore, we can show the following:

Lemma 7.3.2
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e})
. The following are equivalent:

(i)

\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
.

(ii)

For every admissible affinoid cover
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
of
X
satisfying that for each
i\in I
there is an étale map
V_{i}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{K}^{n}
, we have
\mathcal{M}_{|V_{i}}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{V_{i}})_{\Delta}
.

Proof.

Clearly statement
(ii)
implies
(i)
. Thus, let
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
and let
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
be an admissible affinoid cover of
X
as in the statement. By definition of
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
, there is an affinoid cover
(W_{j})_{j\in J}
of
X
such that for each
j\in J
there is an étale map
W_{j}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{n}_{K}
, and
\mathcal{M}_{|W_{j}}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{W_{j}})_{\Delta}
. As
X
is separated, for each
i\in I
the family
(V_{i}\cap W_{j})_{j\in J}
is an admissible affinoid cover of
V_{i}
. Furthermore, as
V_{i}
is affinoid, it is quasi-compact. Hence, we may reduce this to a finite affinoid cover. But then by Corollary
7.2.11
, it follows that
\mathcal{M}_{|V_{i}\cap W_{j}}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{V_{i}\cap W_{j}})_{\Delta}
for each
i\in I
and each
j\in J
. A second application of Corollary
7.2.11
, shows that
\mathcal{M}_{|V_{i}}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{V_{i}})_{\Delta}
.
∎

Proposition 7.3.3
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\in\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e})
. The following are equivalent:

(i)

\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
.

(ii)

There is an admissible cover
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
of
X
such that
\mathcal{M}_{|V_{i}}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{V_{i}})_{\Delta}
for each
i\in I
.

Proof.

This follows at once from Lemma
7.3.2
, as given an admissible cover
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
of
X
, we may refine it by an admissible affinoid cover and, conversely, given admissible affinoid covers of each of the
U_{i}
, the union of all such covers is an admissible affinoid cover of
X
.
∎

Now that we have shown that
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
may be determined locally, we can start studying some of its algebraic properties:

Proposition 7.3.4
.

\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
is an abelian category.

Proof.

As
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
is a subcategory of
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e})
, it suffices to show that it contains kernels and cokernels of maps in
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
, and that every morphism in
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
is strict. This may be shown locally, where we can use Lemma
7.2.8
.
∎

Lemma 7.3.5
.

Let
X
be a smooth affinoid space and
f:U\rightarrow X
be the inclusion of an affinoid subspace. Let
h:Y\rightarrow X
be a closed immersion, and assume we have a factorization
g:Y\rightarrow U
,
h=fg
. Then the pushforward of
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}
-modules:

f_{+}:\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}^{Y}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{U})\rightarrow\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}^{Y}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}),

satisfies the identity
f_{+}=f_{*}
.

Proof.

By assumption, we have
h=fg
. As
f
is an open immersion, it is smooth. Thus, by
[
9
, Lemma 7.8]
, we have
h_{+}=f_{+}g_{+}
. On the other hand,
we always have
h^{!}=g^{!}f^{!}
. By Kashiwara’s equivalence, we have mutually inverse equivalences of triangulated categories:

h_{+}:\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{Y})\leftrightarrows\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}^{Y}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}):h^{!},\quad g_{+}:\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{Y})\leftrightarrows\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}^{Y}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{U}):g^{!}.

Hence, the composition rules described above imply that we have mutually inverse equivalences of triangulated categories:

f_{+}:\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}^{Y}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{U})\leftrightarrows\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}^{Y}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}):f^{!}.

As
f:U\rightarrow X
is an open immersion, for every
\mathcal{M}\in\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
we have:

f^{!}\mathcal{M}:=\mathcal{O}_{U}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}}^{\mathbb{L}}f^{-1}\mathcal{M}[\operatorname{dim}(U)-\operatorname{dim}(X)]=f^{-1}\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{|U}.

Arguing as above, and using Lemma
3.5.3
, we have mutually inverse equivalences:
f_{*}:\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{U})_{Y}\leftrightarrows\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{Y}:f^{-1}
. The fact that
f^{!}=f^{-1}
implies that
f_{+}=f_{*}
.
∎

Theorem 7.3.6
.

Let
X
be a smooth and separated rigid space. The extension functor induces an exact embedding of abelian categories:

\Delta^{E}_{*}:\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}_{X})_{\Delta}.

Furthermore, its essential image is a Serre subcategory of
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}_{X})
.

Proof.

Let
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
be an admissible affinoid cover of
X
such that for each
i\in I
there is an étale map
V_{i}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{n}_{K}
. Then the family
(V_{i}\times V_{j})_{i,j\in I}
yields an admissible affinoid cover of
X^{2}
, and for each
i,j\in I
we have an étale map
V_{i}\times V_{j}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{2n}_{K}
. Let
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
. By Lemma
6.3.3
, it is enough to show that
\left(\Delta_{*}^{E}\mathcal{M}\right)_{|V_{i}\times V_{j}}
is a sheaf of co-admissible
\wideparen{E}_{X|V_{i}\times V_{j}}
-modules for each
i,j\in I
. Choose a pair
i,j\in I
. If
V_{i}\cap V_{j}
is empty, then
\left(\Delta_{*}^{E}\mathcal{M}\right)_{|V_{i}\times V_{j}}=0
. In particular, it is co-admissible. Assume then that
W=V_{i}\cap V_{j}
is non-empty. Then we have a pullback diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{i}\times V_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

where the vertical arrows and
g
are open immersions, and
\Delta
and
f
are closed immersions. By functoriality of the pushforward of sheaves, we know that:

\left(\Delta_{*}^{E}\mathcal{M}\right)_{|V_{i}\times V_{j}}=\Delta^{E}_{*}(\mathcal{M}_{|W})=g_{*}f_{*}^{E}(\mathcal{M}_{|W}),

where
f_{*}^{E}:=\mathcal{O}_{W^{2}}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{p_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{W}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{W}}f_{*}\mathcal{M}_{|W}
.
By Proposition
7.2.9
, it follows that
f_{*}^{E}(\mathcal{M}_{|W})=\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M}(W))
is a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}_{W}
-module supported on the diagonal. Equivalently, the induced complete bornological
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{W^{2}}
-module:

\operatorname{S}^{-1}f_{*}^{E}(\mathcal{M}_{|W})=p_{2}^{-1}\Omega_{W}^{-1}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{W}}f_{*}^{E}(\mathcal{M}_{|W}),

is a co-admissible
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{W^{2}}
-module supported on the diagonal:
\Delta(W)\subset W^{2}\subset V_{i}\times V_{j}
. Thus, there is a co-admissible
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{W}
-module
\mathcal{N}
such that
\operatorname{S}^{-1}f_{*}^{E}(\mathcal{M}_{|W})=f_{+}\mathcal{N}
. As
g
is an open immersion, it is smooth. Hence, by
[
9
, Lemma 7.8]
we have
\Delta_{+}=g_{+}\circ f_{+}
. Thus, by Lemma
7.3.5
, we have:

\displaystyle\Delta_{+}\mathcal{N}=g_{+}f_{+}\mathcal{N}=g_{+}\left(\operatorname{S}^{-1}f_{*}^{E}(\mathcal{M}_{|W})\right)=

\displaystyle g_{*}\left(\operatorname{S}^{-1}f_{*}^{E}(\mathcal{M}_{|W})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g_{*}\left(p_{2}^{-1}\Omega_{W}^{-1}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{W}}f_{*}^{E}(\mathcal{M}_{|W})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p_{2}^{-1}\Omega_{V_{j}}^{-1}\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{V_{j}}}g_{*}\left(f_{*}^{E}(\mathcal{M}_{|W})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{S}^{-1}\Delta_{*}^{E}(\mathcal{M}_{|W}),

where the fourth identity follows by the fact that the fourth and fifth terms have supports contained in the diagonal. As the pushforward of
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}
-modules along a closed immersion preserves co-admissibility, it follows that
\operatorname{S}^{-1}\Delta_{*}^{E}(\mathcal{M}_{|W})=\Delta_{+}\mathcal{N}

is a co-admissible
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{V_{i}\times V_{j}}
-module. Hence,
\Delta_{*}(\mathcal{M}_{|W})
is a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}_{X|V_{i}\times V_{j}}
-module, as we wanted to show. As
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}_{X})_{\Delta}
is a Serre subcategory of
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}_{X})
, it suffices to show that the essential image of
\Delta_{*}:\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{E}_{X})_{\Delta}
is a Serre subcategory. As this can be done locally, it follows by
(iii)
in Corollary
7.2.15
.
∎

As always, we get the corresponding result for the immersion functor:

Corollary 7.3.7
.

There is an exact embedding of abelian categories:

\Delta^{\operatorname{S}}_{*}:\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X^{2}})_{\Delta}.

Furthermore, the essential image of this functor is a Serre subcategory of
\mathcal{C}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X^{2}})_{\Delta}
.

7.4.
C-complexes and the immersion functor

We will now extend the results of the previous sections to
\mathcal{C}
-complexes. As before, we fix a smooth and separated rigid space
X
. We start by defining
\mathcal{C}
-complexes in the category of bimodules:

Definition 7.4.1
.

Choose
C^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}^{e}_{X})
. We make the following definitions:

(i)

We say
C^{\bullet}
is a bi-
\mathcal{C}
-complex if it is a
\mathcal{C}
-complex with respect to its structure as a complex of
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}
-modules and
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}^{\operatorname{op}}_{X}
-modules. We denote the full subcategory of
\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}^{e}_{X})
given by bi-
\mathcal{C}
-complexes by
\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
.

(ii)

We say
C^{\bullet}
is a diagonal
\mathcal{C}
-complex if it is a bi-
\mathcal{C}
-complex such that its cohomology sheaves are co-admissible diagonal
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}
-bimodules. We denote the full subcategory of
\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}^{e}_{X})
given by diagonal
\mathcal{C}
-complexes by
\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
.

We start by showing some basic properties of
\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
and

\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
:

Lemma 7.4.2
.

The category
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
is a Serre subcategory of

\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
.

Proof.

By definition of
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
, we may assume that
X
is affinoid and admits an étale map
X\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{n}_{K}
. Consider a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
:

0\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{3}\rightarrow 0,

and assume that
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{3}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
. We need to show
\mathcal{M}_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
. As
X
is affinoid, we get a strict short exact sequence on global sections:

0\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{1}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{2}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{3}(X)\rightarrow 0.

By Theorem
7.1.11
, we can regard this as a short exact sequence of co-admissible
\wideparen{E}_{X}(X^{2})
-modules. Hence, there is a short exact sequence of
\wideparen{E}_{X}
-modules:

0\rightarrow\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M}_{1}(X))\rightarrow\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M}_{2}(X))\rightarrow\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M}_{3}(X))\rightarrow 0.

As the first and third terms of the sequence have support contained in the diagonal
\Delta(X)\subset X^{2}
, it follows that
\operatorname{Loc}(\mathcal{M}_{2}(X))
also has support contained in the diagonal. However, by Proposition
7.2.5
, this is equivalent to:

\mathcal{M}_{2}(X)=\mathcal{M}_{2}(X)_{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}(X^{2})),

which shows that
\mathcal{M}_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
, as we wanted to show.
∎

Proposition 7.4.3
.

The categories
\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
and

\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
are triangulated subcategories of the derived category
\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}^{e}_{X})
.

Proof.

Notice that the strict monomorphisms:

\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}\rightarrow\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e},\textnormal{ }\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e},

induce strongly exact forgetful functors:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Forget}:\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e})\rightarrow\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{Forget}:\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e})\rightarrow\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}^{\operatorname{op}}_{X}).

These lift to triangulated functors:

\operatorname{Forget}:\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e})\rightarrow\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}),\textnormal{ }\operatorname{Forget}:\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e})\rightarrow\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}^{\operatorname{op}}_{X}).

By
[
9
, Theorem 8.9]
, the categories
\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
and
\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}^{\operatorname{op}}_{X})
are triangulated subcategories of
\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
and
\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}^{\operatorname{op}}_{X})
respectively. Thus,
\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
is a triangulated subcategory of
\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e})
.

Now consider a distinguished triangle:

C_{1}^{\bullet}\rightarrow C_{2}^{\bullet}\rightarrow C_{3}^{\bullet}\rightarrow C_{1}^{\bullet}[1],

such that
C_{1}^{\bullet},C_{3}^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
and
C_{2}^{\bullet}\in\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
. We need to show that
C_{2}^{\bullet}
is an object of
\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
. It is enough to show that
\operatorname{H}^{i}(C_{2}^{\bullet})\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
. For each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have the following exact sequence in
LH(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Ind(Ban}_{K})}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})^{e})
:

\operatorname{H}^{i-1}(C^{\bullet}_{3})\rightarrow\operatorname{H}^{i}(C^{\bullet}_{1})\rightarrow\operatorname{H}^{i}(C^{\bullet}_{2})\rightarrow\operatorname{H}^{i}(C^{\bullet}_{3})\rightarrow\operatorname{H}^{i+1}(C^{\bullet}_{1}).

By assumption, all elements in this sequence are bi-co-admissible
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}
-modules. Hence, we may regard this as an exact sequence in the abelian category
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
. Furthermore, as a consequence of Lemma
7.4.2
,
\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
is a Serre subcategory of

\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})
. Thus,
\operatorname{H}^{i}(C^{\bullet}_{2})\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, and we are done.
∎

Next, we want to understand the behavior of
\mathcal{C}
-complexes with respect to the derived extension functor
\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}
. Let us start with the following lemma:

Lemma 7.4.4
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}^{\operatorname{Bi}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}
. Then there is an isomorphism in
\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{E}_{X})
:

\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}\mathcal{M}\xrightarrow{\cong}\Delta_{*}^{E}\mathcal{M}.

Proof.

By definition of the derived extension functor we have:

\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}\mathcal{M}=I(\wideparen{E}_{X})\tilde{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{p_{1}^{-1}I(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})\tilde{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}I(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})^{\operatorname{op}}}\Delta_{*}I(\mathcal{M}),

where
\tilde{\otimes}
denotes the tensor product in the left heart
\operatorname{Shv}(X^{2},LH(\widehat{\mathcal{B}}c_{K}))
. Thus, we need to show that the canonical map:

\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}\mathcal{M}\rightarrow I(\wideparen{E}_{X})\tilde{\otimes}_{p_{1}^{-1}I(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})\tilde{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}I(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})^{\operatorname{op}}}\Delta_{*}I(\mathcal{M}),

is a quasi-isomorphism, and that the canonical map:

I(\wideparen{E}_{X})\tilde{\otimes}_{p_{1}^{-1}I(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})\tilde{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}I(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})^{\operatorname{op}}}\Delta_{*}I(\mathcal{M})\rightarrow I(\Delta_{*}^{E}\mathcal{M}),

is an isomorphism as well. As these maps are already defined globally, we may as well assume that
X
is affinoid with an étale map
X\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{n}_{K}
. By the properties of the tensor product of sheaves, for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
the cohomology sheaf
\operatorname{H}^{i}(\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}\mathcal{M})
is the sheafification of the presheaf sending an affinoid subdomain
V\subset X^{2}
to:

(48)

\mathcal{F}^{i}(V):=\operatorname{H}^{i}\left(I(\wideparen{E}_{X}(V))\tilde{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{I(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(p_{1}(V))\tilde{\otimes}_{K}I(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(p_{2}(V))^{\operatorname{op}}}I(\mathcal{M})(V\cap\Delta(X))\right).

First, notice that
\mathcal{F}^{i}(V)=0
whenever
V\cap\Delta(X)
is empty. In particular, the
\mathcal{F}^{i}
are supported in
\Delta(X)
, so their sheafifications
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{i}
will be supported at
\Delta(X)
as well. Let us first show that
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{i}=0
for all
i<0
. Let
U\subset X
be an affinoid subdomain, then by definition of the pullback in the category of presheaves we have:

(49)

\Delta^{-1}\mathcal{F}^{i}(U)=\varinjlim_{V}\operatorname{H}^{i}\left(I(\wideparen{E}_{X}(V))\tilde{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{I(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(p_{1}(V))\tilde{\otimes}_{K}I(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(p_{2}(V))^{\operatorname{op}}}I(\mathcal{M})(U)\right),

where the
V\subset X^{2}
are affinoid subdomains satisfying
V\subset U^{2}
and
V\cap\Delta(X)=U
. In this case, notice that for
i=1,2
we have:

U=p_{i}(\Delta(U))\subseteq p_{i}(\Delta(V))\subseteq p_{i}(\Delta(U^{2}))=U.

Hence, we may rewrite the expression in (
49
) as follows:

\Delta^{-1}\mathcal{F}^{i}(U)=\varinjlim_{V}\operatorname{H}^{i}\left(I(\wideparen{E}_{X}(V))\tilde{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{I(\wideparen{E}_{X}(U^{2}))}I(\mathcal{M})(U)\right).

However, by Theorem
7.3.6

\mathcal{M}(U)
, is a co-admissible
\wideparen{E}_{X}(U^{2})
-module, and the transition map
\wideparen{E}_{X}(U^{2})\rightarrow\wideparen{E}_{X}(V)
is
c
-flat. Hence, it follows by
[
9
, Proposition 5.33]
that we have:

\Delta^{-1}\mathcal{F}^{0}(U)=\varinjlim_{V}I(\wideparen{E}_{X}(V)\overrightarrow{\otimes}_{\wideparen{E}_{X}(U^{2})}\mathcal{M}(U))=I(\mathcal{M}(U))=\Delta^{-1}I(\Delta_{*}^{E}\mathcal{M})(U),

and
\Delta^{-1}\mathcal{F}^{i}(U)=0
for all
i<-1
, as we wanted to show.
∎

We now extend this to the category of diagonal
\mathcal{C}
-complexes:

Proposition 7.4.5
.

The derived extension functor induces a triangulated functor:

\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}:\operatorname{D}^{\operatorname{Bi}}_{\mathcal{C}}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})_{\Delta}\rightarrow\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}^{\Delta}}(\wideparen{E}_{X}).

Furthermore,
\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}
is an equivalence onto its essential image, with inverse:

\Delta^{-1}:\operatorname{D}_{\mathcal{C}^{\Delta}}(\wideparen{E}_{X})\rightarrow\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e}).

Thus, for every diagonal
\mathcal{C}
-complex
\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}
there is a canonical isomorphism in
\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}^{e})
:

\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\cong}\Delta^{-1}\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}.

Proof.

Let
C^{\bullet}
be a diagonal
\mathcal{C}
-complex. By construction, we know that
\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}(C^{\bullet})
is an object of
\operatorname{D}(\wideparen{E}_{X})
with cohomology supported on the diagonal. We need to show that it is a
\mathcal{C}
-complex. We know that
\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}(C^{\bullet})
is a
\mathcal{C}
-complex over
\wideparen{E}_{X}
if and only if
\mathbb{L}\Delta_{*}^{\operatorname{S}}(C^{\bullet})
is a
\mathcal{C}
-complex over
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X^{2}}
. Hence, we may use Lemma
7.3.5
, and the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
7.3.6

to reduce the proof to the case where
X=\operatorname{Sp}(A)
, and has an étale map
X\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{n}_{K}
.

First, recall that, by definition, we have that
\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}
is the left derived functor of:

\Delta_{*}^{I(E)}(-):=I(\wideparen{E}_{X})\tilde{\otimes}^{\mathbb{L}}_{p_{1}^{-1}I(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})\tilde{\otimes}_{K}p_{2}^{-1}I(\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X})^{\operatorname{op}}}\Delta_{*}(-).

By definition of a diagonal
\mathcal{C}
-complex, all cohomology groups of
C^{\bullet}
are co-admissible diagonal
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}_{X}
-bimodules. In particular, it follows by Lemma
7.4.4
that all the cohomology groups of
C^{\bullet}
are
\mathbb{L}\Delta^{E}_{*}
-acyclic. Hence, we can use
[
52
, Tag 06XW]
to conclude that for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
